Retail turnarounds are taking hold just in time for the holiday season

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Once-struggling retailers are entering the holiday quarter with improved performance and sales momentum as turnaround efforts begin to resonate with shoppers.

A handful of earnings reports on Tuesday pointed to a mix of factors behind the strong results, including a resilient consumer, stronger execution and demand for the right products at the right price.

Kohl's reported comparable sales growth of 1.2% for the period ended Oct. 31 and raised its full-year outlook as CEO Michael Bender said customers "have remained resilient" but are "migrating to value with their purchases."

He tied improvement to the company's work on product assortment, store experience and value.

Best Buy also on Tuesday posted its highest comparable sales growth in five years, at 6.1%, which prompted the electronics retailer to raise its full-year outlook.

"We have a tremendous amount of momentum," CEO Jason Bonfig told CNBC in an interview. "The fact that we saw pretty much broad-based growth across almost all of our categories is a lot of momentum that we're very excited about."

The company also credited execution, market share gains and a compelling product cycle alongside growing customer demand.

At Dick's Sporting Goods, quarterly comparable sales rose 2.6% at the core business and 0.5% at Foot Locker. CEO Lauren Hobart said "all income demographics grew" and the company "didn't see trade-down from best to better or better to good."

She similarly pointed to new products and market share gains within the category as contributing to the strong quarter. She also highlighted continued improvement at Foot Locker.

All three companies have been undergoing efforts to boost sales and get the right products on shelves to meet customer demand.

Target, likewise undergoing revitalization efforts, last week reported comparable sales growth of 5.6% in the third quarter, fueled by a 5% increase in comparable traffic. The retailer raised its full-year outlook.

CEO Michael Fiddelke at the time called the traffic gain "a healthy sign that guests are responding to our changes and choosing Target more often."

Even Gap CEO Richard Dickson told CNBC the company has moved from "rebuild mode" to "turnaround mode" at its Old Navy banner, a sign of progress, albeit not a broad-based portfolio recovery.

Michael Baker, a retail analyst at D.A. Davidson, told CNBC he sees both a resilient consumer and company-specific improvements as driving sector strength.

"Retail sales have been more resilient than most think," Baker said. His analysis showed retail sales, excluding autos and gas, rose 5.1% in the third quarter on a nonseasonally adjusted basis — the strongest quarter since the pandemic.

But he cautioned that growth appears to be coming more from transaction size than traffic, with inflation, promotions and higher units per transaction driving up spend.

Dollar Tree, which reported better-than-expected earnings on Tuesday morning, posted a 3.8% increase to traffic, accelerating from 0.4% growth in the prior quarter, suggesting more shoppers may be bargain hunting. Comparable sales at the retailer increased 4.6% during the period.

"Consumers are under pressure, and they are increasingly selective about where and how they spend," said Dollar Tree CEO Michael Creedon on the company's earnings call. "When under pressure, shoppers prioritize value and buy closer to need."

Comparable sales of everyday essentials rose 8.9% at Dollar Tree stores in the third quarter, while sales of nonessential items were essentially flat, the company said.

— CNBC's Laya Neelakandan contributed to this report.

Jensen Huang apologized to Nvidia employees and said he is in therapy

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Nvidia's CEO told staff he violated the company's code of conduct, a day after the board reinstated him

Nvidia $NVDA CEO Jensen Huang apologized to employees at an all-hands meeting on Tuesday and told them he is in therapy, according to The Wall Street Journal.

Huang called it "the hardest company meeting" he had held, according to Bloomberg. He admitted to employees that he had failed them, owned up to breaking Nvidia's code of conduct and said he appreciated being given another opportunity to run the business he helped start.

Nvidia's board reinstated Huang as president and CEO and as a board member on Monday, the company said. The vote was unanimous and followed a series of meetings with Huang, after which the board was satisfied with his candor and ability to lead. Senior U.S. government officials gave the board input, and the board is confident his return poses no national security risk, the company said. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress had served as interim CEO while keeping her CFO duties.

Huang credited the Trump administration with speedily sharing intelligence with Nvidia, which he said allowed the board to conclude that the dangers of bringing him back were small, according to The Wall Street Journal.

He said the board reached its decision after reviewing the evidence and getting assurance that national security was not at risk, and that the wishes of top government officials to have him back did not influence it, according to Bloomberg. Huang said President Donald Trump loves Nvidia. "He is my president," Huang said. "I am in service of making our president — our country — successful."

Huang focused on the code of conduct during the meeting, according to The Wall Street Journal. On Sunday, he told the board that there had been more affairs than the two widely reported in the media. Those two involved an employee and an executive of a company that buys Nvidia's products, a person U.S. officials suspected of acting as an asset for Chinese intelligence.

Nvidia said a board committee of independent directors is investigating reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, and that the inquiry will proceed to conclusion. Huang supports the investigation and is committed to cooperating with it, the company said.

Huang took no questions from employees at the meeting, according to Bloomberg. Nvidia stock fell more than 7% in Tuesday trading after a rally on Monday.

The second issue is this: in a complex trust that has DNI that contains both interest income and capital gains, and the trust has a sole beneficiary, can the trustee be given discretion to distribute to the beneficiary an amount that contains only capital gains? The answer appears to be NO. Per Sec 662(b) and Reg 1.662(b)-1 the income distribution to the beneficiary retains the same tax character on a pro-rata basis that it had within the trust

c) 2026 Qualified dividends = $100
d) Total cash distributed to beneficiary = $200

$186.92 LTCG on line 9a

$6.23 Qualified dividends on line 6b

One of the world’s biggest zinc mines is getting Alaska’s largest solar farm

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Red Dog Mine in Alaska burns about 40,000 gallons of diesel a day to keep one of the world’s largest zinc mines running, and now, an 8.8-megawatt (MW) solar farm and battery storage will help cut that fuel use.

The Alaska Energy Authority’s board unanimously approved a nearly $5 million loan for the project through its Power Project Fund. Tugliq Red Dog1 will build, own, and operate the roughly $35 million solar project and sell the electricity to mine operator Teck Alaska. The state’s loan review says site work began in June and expects the solar and storage to come online in October 2028.

Red Dog sits about 105 miles north of the Arctic Circle, on land owned by NANA, an Iñupiat-owned Alaska Native corporation. The array would be larger than any solar farm operating in Alaska today, as KOTZ’s Desiree Hagen reported for Alaska Public Media. Hagen reported that the plans call for more than 15,000 panels across around 30 acres.

The mine gets all its power from on-site diesel generators. In summer, it can draw so much electricity that Teck has to cut power elsewhere at the site. The solar array and the 8.8 MW, 15 megawatt-hour (MWh) battery will give the operator another source of power during those crunches.

The state of Alaska expects the solar and storage project to cover about 5% of the zinc mine’s electricity needs under its current operating plan. The project could do even more after mining ends, covering about 90% of the site’s annual electricity needs.

Teck expects the mine’s existing deposits to support operations through 2031, and it’s studying whether to develop two nearby deposits. If that expansion goes ahead, the state projects the solar and battery project would cover about 3% of the larger operation’s electricity needs. If mining ends, demand will drop sharply, but the site will still need electricity for cleanup and long-term water treatment.

Join Target Circle free, then enter your phone number or scan the Target app’s Wallet barcode at every checkout, since its deals come off automatically only for members who identify themselves. Etihad’s New Credit Cards Are Primarily for Status Chasers

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site or when you click to or take an action on their website. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

The new cards offer perks like faster paths to status and elevated earning rates when purchasing tickets with partners.

Etihad Guest, the loyalty program of Abu Dhabi-based Etihad Airways, is expanding its U.S. presence by introducing two new credit cards. Most people won’t get much value from these cards, but they can make sense for Etihad frequent flyers who want elite status.

The two cards are the Etihad Guest Visa Signature® Card ($99 annual fee) and the Etihad Guest Premier Visa Signature® Card ($499 annual fee). Issued by the First National Bank of Omaha, both offer faster pathways to earning and maintaining elite status and the ability to earn extra miles on Etihad purchases.

They also offer extra miles on international transactions (neither has foreign transaction fees) and purchases with Etihad’s partner airlines, which include U.S. carriers like American Airlines and JetBlue Airways.

Here’s what you need to know about the cards.

The Etihad Guest Visa Signature® Card comes with a $99 annual fee, and it earns Etihad Guest miles at the following rates:

3 miles per $1 spent on Etihad purchases.

2 miles per $1 spent on Etihad partner airline purchases.

2 miles per $1 spent on non-U.S. purchases.

1 mile per $1 spent on all other U.S. purchases.

Welcome offer: Through Feb. 3, 2027, the card is offering the following welcome offer for new applicants: Earn up to 40,000 welcome Etihad Guest Miles, including 10,000 miles after the first purchase and an additional 30,000 miles after spending $3,000 within the first three billing cycles after account opening.

Elite status benefits: You’ll also get Etihad Silver status after your first Etihad purchase within 90 days of opening your account. Unlike most airlines, that entry-level status is enough to get you into the business class lounge when traveling through Etihad’s hub at Abu Dhabi’s Zayed International Airport.

You can retain that status by spending $20,000 a year on the card, or $3,000 a year on Etihad purchases.

The Etihad Guest Premier Visa Signature® Card comes with a $499 annual fee, and it earns Etihad Guest miles at the following rates:

5 miles per $1 spent on Etihad purchases.

3 miles per $1 spent on Etihad partner airline purchases.

2 miles per $1 spent on non-U.S. purchases.

1 mile per $1 spent on all other U.S. purchases.

The only differences in rewards between the premium and standard cards are increased earning rates for eligible airline purchases.

Welcome offer: Through Feb. 3, 2027, the premium card offers a higher welcome offer compared to the standard card: Earn up to 60,000 welcome Etihad Guest Miles, including 20,000 miles after the first purchase and 40,000 miles after spending $7,000 within the first three billing cycles after account opening.

Elite status benefits: The premium card offers a faster track to elite status: automatic Silver status upon card approval and Gold status after your first Etihad purchase within 90 days of opening your account.

To maintain Gold status, you’ll need to spend $40,000 a year on the card, or $8,000 on Etihad purchases. Otherwise, you’ll have Silver status.

Wi-Fi benefit: After your first Etihad purchase, you’ll get 3,000 Etihad Guest miles, enough to cover Wi-Fi costs on a long-haul Etihad flight. You’ll then get those miles again after each cardholder anniversary.

Travel benefits: The card offers a statement credit for Global Entry or TSA PreCheck and select travel protections. If you already have a premium travel card, you might already have access to these perks.

The card also includes a Priority Pass lounge access membership. However, an Etihad spokesperson told NerdWallet that it’s the entry-level membership tier, so you’ll need to pay for yourself and guests each time you visit a Priority Pass lounge. Most premium credit cards in the U.S. that offer Priority Pass allow complimentary visits for at least the cardholder.

The Etihad cards make the most sense for Etihad’s frequent flyers who are flying to or connecting through Abu Dhabi several times a year.

That’s because the standard card’s accelerated pathway to Etihad Guest’s Silver status tier can unlock the ability to choose business class lounge access in Abu Dhabi as a status perk.

With the premium card, you get a fast track to Gold. If you select lounge access as a Gold status perk, you can not only bring a guest on the same flight into the lounge, but also access Etihad’s worldwide lounge network. That includes Etihad’s own lounges in New York and Washington, D.C., as well as most partner lounges at other Etihad destinations.

Bear in mind that while Etihad has many partnerships with other airlines, it’s not part of an alliance, so you don’t get lounge benefits when traveling on partners. You’ll need to be flying on an Etihad-operated flight to get into the lounge.

If you primarily fly with other carriers, then Etihad status will have limited upside for you.

However, the Priority Pass lounge membership that comes with the premium card does not have that restriction. You can access Priority Pass lounges while flying on any airline, although you’ll need to pay an additional per-person $35 fee each visit.

The earning rates on the Etihad cards are lackluster for everyday spenders. The bonus categories are for airline purchases with Etihad and its partners, as well as international spending.

That means your day-to-day purchases at home, like groceries, dining out and gas, earn only 1 mile per $1 spent.

Plus, there are more ways to earn Etihad Guest miles than spending on an Etihad credit card. For example, you can transfer points to Etihad Guest from Bilt Rewards, Capital One and Citi ThankYou.

Those programs offer cards that typically have better benefits and bonus categories, and they offer flexibility on how you can use your points and miles. With an Etihad card, you can only earn Etihad Guest miles, which expire after 18 months without qualifying flight activity. (If you’re not traveling on Etihad or its partners at least once every 18 months, then you definitely should not get the cards.)

One of the most interesting perks of the new cards is the bonus category for spending with Etihad partner airlines — 2 miles per $1 for the standard card and 3 miles per $1 for the premium version. Typically, airline credit cards reward customers only for spending on the airline, not partners.

There are better cards available, though. Some offer as many or even more points per $1 on flights purchased directly from airlines.

If you want the card solely to earn more Etihad Guest miles, then the answer for most people is no.

While you can earn more miles for purchases with Etihad and partner airlines, the earning rates aren’t attractive enough to justify the annual fees, especially when compared with existing cards with currencies that transfer to Etihad Guest.

Status chasers could benefit from the fast track to elite status — but you would need to be a fairly frequent Etihad flyer to really take advantage of your elite benefits.

You want a travel credit card that prioritizes what’s important to you. Here are some of the best travel credit cards of 2026:

Why Ultra-Long-Haul Flights Are Easier On An Aircraft Than Short Hops

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Services like Qatar Airways' flight from Doha to Auckland and Singapore Airlines' route from Singapore to New York are among the most challenging flights in the world. They push modern aircraft to their limits, testing their range, performance, and fuel burn like no other. There's been a noticeable increase in ultra-long-haul flights in recent years, driven by the arrival of new aircraft like the Airbus A350 and Boeing 787 Dreamliner, which are more capable and efficient than earlier widebodies.

However, while these flights push planes to their technical limits, they aren't particularly physically demanding on the aircraft. Longer flights are actually much less stressful on the airframe and the engines than operating multiple shorter services in a given day with few breaks in between. Naturally, multiple factors go into how stressed an aircraft is during a flight, and time between flights also matters.

The lifespan of large transport aircraft is really determined by two measurements: cycles and hours. The hours metric is as the word implies, while cycles measure the number of times an aircraft is pressurized and depressurized. This roughly corresponds to the number of flights the aircraft takes, although airliners do occasionally operate flights without fully pressurizing the cabin (these are almost always ferry flights flown when the aircraft has a pressurization issue).

Widebody aircraft operating long-haul and ultra-long-haul flights tend to rack up hours very quickly, while short-haul airliners rack up cycles quickly. However, while hours are one measure of an airframe's lifespan, the number of cycles/flights operated is a more important metric for determining how stressed the aircraft is overall. Operating multiple flights means the landing gear and brakes are used for multiple landings, while the fuel and hydraulic systems also wear with each flight.

In addition, each flight involves the engines being pushed to takeoff power and, typically, the reverse thrusters being activated upon landing (this doesn't occur with every landing, but it occurs onalmost every landing). This puts strain on the engines, whereas an aircraft operating a long-haul or ultra-long-haul flight only performs one takeoff and one landing. The number of hours an aircraft flies certainly stresses the airframe, but its cycles and the number of flights it takes physically stress the plane even more.

Hawaiian Airlines currently operates 19 717-200s. These planes are used exclusively to operate between the Hawaiian islands, which means that they routinely operate eight, nine, 10, or even 11 flights per day. Most of these flights last under 40 minutes, so they're not racking up hours as quickly as a typical widebody. However, despite the short length of these routes, the Hawaiian interisland operations are considered some of the most demanding in the world.

The aircraft's engines are routinely pushed to takeoff power, then reverse thrust is used on the runway, and then this process is repeated multiple times per day. The engines are constantly being used and rarely have time to cool down. The landing gear and brakes are used frequently, and short turnaround times mean the plane rarely rests. What's more, the environment exposes the aircraft to salt particles in the air, raising corrosion concerns and increasing stress on the airframe.

737 MAX 7, 737 MAX 8, 737 MAX 9, 737 MAX 10

One of Hawaiian's biggest challenges was determining how these aging planes would be replaced. The engines on newer types like the A321neo and 737 MAX would not take kindly to the constant stress, while their fuel efficiency would provide little benefit. Ultimately, Alaska Air Group (which now owns the Hawaiian brand) opted to use older 737-800s from Alaska's fleet to replace the 717s, presumably scheduling extra downtime due to the aircraft's size and to allow the planes to rest more.

The A350's reputation as a 'passenger favorite' masks a more complex truth for flight crews, whose rest experiences can vary widely.

Hour and cycle limits are mainly used for pressurized aircraft because of the stresses pressurization introduces on the airframe. While other components of the aircraft also get stressed, pressurization is one of the largest stress factors. Each time the aircraft is pressurized, the fuselage expands slightly as the high pressure air inside the cabin pushes the fuselage against the low-pressure air outside. Over time, this can fatigue the fuselage, potentially resulting in microscopic cracks.

Regularly scheduled maintenance inspections uncover any potential fatigue cracks before they become a safety issue, but the pressurization cycle nonetheless puts significant stress on the fuselage. The pressurization cycle and these stress forces were a major factor in two mid-flight breakups of the de Havilland Comet in the 1950s, the world's first jet airliner. While it was not the first pressurized aircraft, no other airliner flew so high, and the intense forces from pressurization cycles fatigued the fuselage far faster than anticipated.

Metallic fuselages are particularly susceptible to the stress forces caused by pressurization, while newer carbon-composite designs are much stronger. This has allowed manufacturers to lower cabin altitudes by using higher-pressure air, which would ordinarily require a much heavier metal fuselage due to the added strengthening needed. Meanwhile, some business jets have even lower cabin altitudes while flying higher than airliners, which is justifiable as weight and fuel burn are less of a concern in private aviation.

Aircraft are designed to serve for upwards of 30 or even 35 years, so aircraft engineers design them with a specific service life in mind that covers typical usage across multiple decades. Manufacturers perform fatigue analysis to establish safe limits and inspection intervals for the aircraft, while working with regulators to determine the aircraft's lifespan. This is designed around the plane's intended mission, with widebodies and narrowbodies having vastly different hours-to-cycles ratios.

In addition to the aircraft's overall lifespan, various components may have their own maintenance intervals, with engines in particular largely on their own schedules. Overall, aircraft designers set goals for the expected service life of the aircraft and its components, and then work diligently to achieve them. In addition, the original limits set for an aircraft are often increased by the manufacturer later in the type's service life, usually via an upgrade that can be retrofitted onto pre-existing examples.

The A320 was originally designed with a service limit of 60,000 flight hours or 48,000 cycles, whichever occurs first. Later on, Airbus was able to increase these limits to 120,000 hours or 60,000 cycles, whichever occurs first. The 767, meanwhile, is designed with a limit of 50,000 cycles, fewer than the A320, but more hours. In addition, some aircraft types are converted to freighters later in their lives, and these projects often come with post-conversion limits as well.

Is there a possibility that Boeing will make an extended-range version of the 787 Dreamliner?

An aircraft's age is often quoted in terms of years, but this doesn't have much of an impact on its actual remaining lifespan. While age can determine factors such as reliability and which parts need to be used, planes can easily operate for upwards of 40 years if they don't exceed their hour and cycle limits. Instead, however, planes are largely retired for economic reasons, as maintaining an old aircraft that has operated for decades is possible but rarely cheap.

As aircraft become older, they become less reliable, and maintenance costs tend to rise. In addition, airliners require multiple types of maintenance overhauls, including what are often dubbed 'D checks', where basically the entire aircraft is taken apart, which come at a huge cost. Older aircraft also burn more fuel than newer ones, which makes it much more expensive to operate aging planes. Oftentimes, airlines will retire their oldest planes right before their next major overhaul, and then they get sent for scrapping.

Because aircraft are retired for economic reasons, it's actually rare to see them near their service limitations. What's more, aging planes tend to be used less often towards the end of their lives due to their high operating costs, which further slows the pace at which the aircraft approaches its service limits. As such, some aircraft remain in service for upwards of 40 years with small cargo operators and in nations such as Iran, as well as older 737s and A320s that are nearly 40 years old, since they remain within their hour and cycle limits.

Your Credit Report Shows How You Borrow – Not What You Earn or Save

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Your credit report lists your loans and cards with their balances, limits, and payment history, plus any collections, any bankruptcy, and the companies that have checked it. Your income, savings, and credit score aren’t on it. Pull your free reports and read each section for accounts you didn’t open, wrong balances, and on-time payments marked late.

Lenders read that report before they approve you or set your rate, and landlords, insurers, and phone companies can check it too. Seeing it first lets you catch a mistake before it costs you.

The report covers credit only. Paying with a debit card isn’t borrowing, so that spending normally doesn’t show up. Your score is calculated from the report but isn’t part of it. Since April 2023, the three credit bureaus have also stopped listing any medical bill sent to collectors that’s been paid off, was under $500 when first reported, or is less than a year past due.

Negative items don’t stay for good. The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau says most, like late payments, can be reported for up to seven years, and a bankruptcy for up to 10.

Read each of your free reports one section at a time:

Not every surprise is a problem. A first-time reader might find a store card she forgot, still open at a $0 balance. That card has been helping her, since an open account with no missed payments keeps adding to the length of her credit history.

Dispute any entry that’s wrong, not just forgotten.

Checking your own reports never lowers your score, so read them now, well before a lender does.

Amazon Resale Sells Returns for Less – Check What the Grade Says Is Missing

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Before you buy a bigger item new on Amazon, check Amazon Resale, formerly Amazon Warehouse. Amazon sells inspected returns and open-box goods there below the new price, with its usual return policy. Read the condition grade first, since a lower grade can mean missing accessories or parts, and buy only when the discount covers their cost.

One product can be listed on Resale in several grades at once, each at its own price.

Amazon inspects each item before giving it one of four grades, and it tests electronics and resets them to factory settings. A Like New item works perfectly and has every essential accessory, though its packaging may be damaged. A Very Good one may show light use and minor cosmetic flaws, and it can lack non-essential accessories.

A Good item can show wear from moderate use, and an Acceptable one can show clear signs of use, such as scratches and dents. Either may be missing accessories or parts you’d have to buy before you can use it, and the listing’s description names anything missing.

To find offers, browse Amazon Resale by category, or look on a regular product page for the Save with Used or Buy Used section and open See details to compare grades. Resale purchases fall under Amazon’s return policy, which gives most items 30 days. Each item is one of a kind, though, so Amazon can’t swap a disappointing one for another in exactly the same condition. Test your item the day it arrives.

Check the maker’s warranty too. Under FTC rules, a written warranty on a product over $15 must say so if it covers only the original buyer, which may not be you. For a laptop or phone you’d want covered by a warranty, look at refurbished electronics sold with one instead.

Say a stand mixer is listed as Like New at 20% below the new price because a buyer opened the box and sent it back. Every essential accessory is included, so you get a fully working mixer for 20% less, in an opened box. At a lower grade with the bowl missing, the discount has to cover a new bowl before it saves you anything.

Like New and Very Good are the grades to start with, since neither can be missing anything the item needs to work.

Search Amazon Resale for the next bigger item on your shopping list.

Cheaper Pet Food Can Be as Complete as Premium – Check the AAFCO Statement

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Before paying extra for premium dog or cat food, find the small-print line on the package that names your pet’s life stage and AAFCO, the group that sets pet food nutrient standards. Any food with that line meets the same standards as pricier brands, so buy the cheapest one your pet does well on.

In each food’s biggest bag at Walmart, the store’s own Pure Balance Chicken & Brown Rice Recipe costs $1.17 a pound, less than half the $2.67 a pound for Blue Buffalo’s Wilderness Grain-Free Chicken Recipe. Both dry dog foods carry that line and have nearly the same calories per pound.

That line is the nutritional adequacy statement, and newer labels add a short version on the front, such as Complete Food for Adult Dogs. The statement says the food was formulated to meet the nutrient standards of the Association of American Feed Control Officials (AAFCO) or passed AAFCO feeding trials. It also names a life stage, such as growth for puppies and kittens, adult maintenance, or all life stages. The FDA says a food has to pass one of those tests to be called complete and balanced, which means it’s meant to be your pet’s only food. One labeled for intermittent or supplemental feeding only isn’t a complete diet.

Premium claims don’t make a food more complete. Natural describes how ingredients are sourced and processed, organic how they’re grown, and human-grade how the food and its ingredients are handled. Grain-free isn’t healthier for most pets either. Veterinary nutritionists at Tufts University call it a marketing concept and say grain allergies are rare.

The FDA has also investigated reports of a heart disease called dilated cardiomyopathy in dogs eating certain foods, many of them grain-free. It hasn’t established that the foods cause the disease, but ask your vet before switching your dog to grain-free.

So compare prices only among foods whose statement names your pet’s life stage, and look at dry food first. In a Tufts price check of cat foods, even the most expensive dry food cost less per day than the cheapest canned one, for the same calories.

If you’d rather cook, have a veterinary nutritionist write the recipe for any homemade dog food or cat food you’d serve daily.

For a grown dog or cat, a store brand whose statement covers adult maintenance or all life stages is a complete diet.

6th Circuit upholds jury win for Ohio State in former graduate student’s sexual harassment lawsuit

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

While the plaintiff argued the district court incorrectly instructed the jury, the 6th Circuit said she’d failed to raise these objections at the proper time.

A former Ohio State University graduate student could not convince a federal appeals court to overturn a jury’s verdict in favor of the school in her sexual harassment lawsuit because she failed to show that her trial was unfairly prejudiced, according to a Tuesday decision.

The plaintiff in Huang v. The Ohio State University alleged that a faculty advisor subjected her to unwanted touching and that, when she resisted his advances, he retaliated by manipulating her candidacy exam for an engineering doctorate. She brought claims under Title VII of the 1964 Civil Rights Act as well as the 14th Amendment’s due process right to bodily integrity.

A district granted summary judgment for Ohio State on the plaintiff’s Title VII claims, holding that she was not an employee of the university within the law’s definition and therefore could not bring those claims. At trial, a jury held for the plaintiff’s advisor. She appealed to the 6th U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals, which reversed and ordered a new trial.

At the second trial, the jury determined that the plaintiff was not an employee because Ohio State and the advisor did not have the “right to control the manner and means by which [the plaintiff] accomplished her work.” The jury also ruled in favor of the advisor on the plaintiff’s 14th Amendment claim.

The plaintiff again appealed to the 6th Circuit, alleging that the district court incorrectly instructed the jury on what facts were relevant to its determination of whether she was an employee. She also claimed that the court abused its discretion in excluding certain evidence and that it improperly prohibited her from requesting economic damages in her closing argument.

Each of these claims failed, the 6th Circuit held.

The plaintiff forfeited her challenge to the court’s jury instructions because she failed to raise it, despite the district court specifically asking whether the parties had objections or edits to its instructions. To grant a third trial on this point “would be unfair to the court and the defendants,” the 6th Circuit said.

In her evidentiary argument, the plaintiff claimed that the district court improperly prevented her from submitting portions of a slideshow containing the results of an Ohio State campus climate survey on sexual assault. She argued that the presentation would have made her allegations more plausible.

However, the plaintiff failed to include the relevant slides in her appeal, leaving the 6th Circuit unable to conclude whether they had sufficient weight to affect the jury’s verdict. The appeals court also could not determine whether the slides offered anything “above and beyond” other testimony in the case.

In all, the plaintiff failed to show that any of her allegations amounted to unfair prejudice warranting a new trial.

The case represents one in a handful of recent jury decisions to favor employers facing Title VII lawsuits. In March, a jury held for Haribo of America in a former employee’s discrimination and retaliation case, determining that the plaintiff’s dismissal was a mutually agreed-upon separation between the parties rather than retaliation.

And in a 2025 case, a jury sided with Levi Strauss in a lawsuit alleging that a former executive did not receive a promotion because of her pregnancy, gender and age.

JSX Buys Bankrupt Airline In Major Expansion Beyond 30-Seat Semi-Private Flights

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JSX has acquired bankrupt New Pacific Airlines, securing a regulatory pathway to operate aircraft with more than 30 passenger seats. The airline, which abandoned its ambitious Boeing 757 operation in November 2025, was acquired in August 2026, although the deal only became public following a November 20 Department of Transportation (DOT) filing, as reported by AeroTime.

Rather than introducing new aircraft, JSX intends to transfer existing Embraer ERJ145-LRs into the acquired airline, increasing seating capacity from 30 to 42 passengers. The acquisition also creates opportunities beyond its immediate plans, potentially including higher-capacity turboprops, while providing greater flexibility amid ongoing regulatory scrutiny of JSX's operating model.

JSX reportedly acquired New Pacific for approximately $3.1 million in consideration. According to the November 20 DOT filing, the airline intends to restart operations under the JSX Air brand using two ERJ145-LRs leased from sister company Delux Public Charter, increasing to six aircraft by the fifth month. Projections include 10,106 flights and approximately 309,000 passenger boardings during the first year.

The key acquisition is New Pacific's FAA Part 121 operating certificate. JSX currently flies under Part 135, selling individual seats through the Part 380 public charter framework. This limits its aircraft to 30 passenger seats, even though its Embraer jets were originally designed to carry more. New Pacific's certificate provides a pathway to operate 42-seat aircraft, subject to regulatory approvals.

The move also offers regulatory flexibility. AP New previously reported how the FAA has proposed tightening oversight of public charter operators, following criticism from major airlines and pilot unions. Although those changes remain unresolved, a Part 121 operation could provide JSX with an alternative growth platform. Responding to questions about the acquisition, the company told View From The Wing:

"JSX is continually evaluating opportunities to support its long-term growth and bring its distinctive flying experience to more customers and more communities. As part of that work, we are pursuing additional pathways that could provide even more ways to deliver our award-winning service in the future."

Originally known as Northern Pacific Airways, the airline planned to connect North America with Japan and South Korea through Anchorage, using Boeing 757-200 aircraft. Inspired by Icelandair's connecting-hub model, the strategy unraveled following Russian airspace restrictions in 2022. The carrier lacked the ETOPS approval required for alternative routings, forcing management to seek other markets.

In July 2023, the airline instead launched domestic flights between Ontario, California, and Las Vegas, subsequently adding Reno and Nashville. Rebranded New Pacific Airlines in September 2023, it abandoned scheduled operations in April 2024 to focus on charter flights. Management then pursued corporate and sports-team charters, converting its Boeing 757s from 181 seats to just 78 premium seats.

Russian airspace restrictions disrupt plans

Flying ends; approximately 115 employees laid off

However, neither business model proved sustainable. According to January 2026 bankruptcy documents, the wider group burned approximately $5 million monthly during its earlier difficulties, while the NHL contract initially lost money on every flight. Expensive Boeing 757 maintenance and disputed payments worsened liquidity problems. Operations ceased in November last year, and the airline's Part 121 certificate became an important asset in bankruptcy proceedings.

The end of an era for a carrier that experienced more than its fair share of struggles.

JSX's immediate plans involve six 42-seat ERJ145s, where it can increase capacity per aircraft by 40% over the current 30-seat layout. Paxex.aero reports that proposed routes would average 230–345 miles (370–556 km), with approximately six daily sectors per aircraft. Higher capacity could improve economics, although retaining JSX's spacious cabin, currently featuring individual seats and side tables on some aircraft, may become challenging.

The acquisition could also benefit JSX's turboprop strategy. The airline introduced ATR 42-600 flights in December 2025 with 30 premium seats per aircraft, and has outlined plans for up to 25 ATR aircraft, potentially including the larger ATR 72-600. Under Part 121, both types could theoretically accommodate substantially more passengers, provided New Pacific secures the necessary aircraft-specific operating approvals.

Only the 42-seat ERJ145 configuration has been announced. Higher-capacity ATR operations remain hypothetical.

Ultimately, JSX could develop two complementary offerings: its established 30-seat premium service and higher-capacity flights under Part 121. Its expanding ATR operation already demonstrates a willingness to diversify aircraft types, while New Pacific's certificate offers flexibility to accommodate further growth. The acquisition therefore gives JSX more than additional seats: it provides an alternative regulatory platform without requiring the airline to abandon its existing business model.

Air New Zealand Denied All Slots For New 27-Hour Route: Here's Why

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Air New Zealand has been looking to return to the UK market for some time. The last time we saw its black-and-white livery was in March 2020, when it operated its final flight from London Heathrow Airport (LHR) to Auckland International Airport (AKL). Since then, passengers traveling between the UK and New Zealand have had to rely on connecting flights through hubs in the Middle East, Asia or Australia.

That being said, for the upcoming summer 2027 season, the New Zealand flag carrier had sought to secure slots at Heathrow. According to Airport Coordination Limited's (ACL) Initial Coordination Report for London Heathrow, the Star Alliance member requested 434 slots for the season. However, none of these were approved.

Air New Zealand had been flying to London for several decades. The airline initially served London Gatwick Airport (LGW) from Auckland, with stops in Papeete, Tahiti, and Los Angeles. In 1994, it moved its operations to Heathrow, where it continued to fly via Los Angeles. Between 2006 and 2013, the airline also offered flights via Hong Kong.

However, the Star Alliance member eventually decided to discontinue its London service due to low passenger numbers. It is unclear what routing it would have used had it secured the slots for summer 2027. Los Angeles appears to have been one possibility, as a spokesperson previously told Simple Flying that the airline would most likely operate the service via the US city using Boeing 777 aircraft.

Interestingly, Air New Zealand had also applied for slots at London Gatwick for the previous summer season and was actually successful in securing them. However, the airline never launched the flights, as it was facing aircraft availability issues at the time. Up to four of its 14 Boeing 787-9 Dreamliners were grounded simultaneously due to problems with Rolls-Royce Trent 1000 engines and maintenance delays.

These aircraft have since returned to service. Nevertheless, aircraft availability remains a concern. According to ch-aviation, Air New Zealand currently has nine 777-300ERs and 16 787-9s, although only 11 of the latter are in active service. The airline is also awaiting deliveries of three additional 787-9s and five 787-10s, with its outstanding Dreamliner orders expected to be fulfilled by 2028.

However, Boeing has faced difficulties increasing 787 production to its target of ten aircraft per month, partly due to supply-chain delays involving General Electric GEnx engines. These issues have affected deliveries to customers, including Air New Zealand, although planespotters.net data shows that the airline received two Dreamliners in October 2026.

Apart from new aircraft deliveries, the Star Alliance member is also refurbishing its existing widebody fleet. As of late October, 13 of its 14 787-9s had completed cabin upgrades, which include replacing the older inward-facing Business Premier seats with private suites, updating Economy cabins and introducing bunk-style sleeping pods on selected aircraft.

The final Dreamliner is expected to return to service before the end of the year. The airline is also scheduled to begin refurbishing its 777 fleet in March 2027. All of which are expected to undergo the upgrades by the end of 2027, meaning aircraft will need to be temporarily withdrawn from service while the work takes place.

Air New Zealand is one of the principal airlines operating in the South Pacific, with a fleet for NZ's domestic needs and ultra-long-haul routes.

The exact reason why Air New Zealand did not receive any slots has not been confirmed. However, Heathrow is one of the most slot-constrained airports in the world, and securing additional slots is difficult. According to ACL's report, airlines requested a total of 26,832 slots for summer 2027, but only 151 were allocated, just 0.6% of the requests.

Xiamen Air, which recently launched a weekly service between Xiamen and Heathrow, received most of the approved slots. However, several other airlines were unsuccessful. These included Alaska Airlines, which only began flying to Heathrow from Seattle in May and requested 434 slots for next summer. Emirates also failed to secure any of the 1,920 slots it requested, while Virgin Atlantic, Delta Air Lines, Air India and Riyadh Air were denied all 1,798, 556, 1,736 and 868 slots they sought, respectively.

Burning Man CEO Marian Goodell to Exit: ‘I’m Not Tired, I’m Not Done — I’m Ready to Be a Mentor’

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Burning Man's CEO since 2013, Goodell will remain in the role during the search for a new leader and keep her seat on the Burning Man board.

Burning Man Project’s longtime CEO Marian Goodell has announced that she’s leaving the position.

Goodell will remain as CEO during the search for a new leader of Burning Man Project, the nonprofit organization behind the annual gathering in Nevada’s Black Rock Desert that also supports the global Burning Man community, including the worldwide network of official Regional Burning Man Events. Goodell will remain on the Burning Man board after stepping down.

Goodell tells Billboard that while there is no set goal date to have someone new in the CEO role, she’s “fairly confident that having somebody in place at the beginning of the year — so they can spend some time with me — is a reasonable expectation.” She says that as one of the co-founders of Burning Man, she’s keen to help mentor a new CEO who will serve as the next generation of leadership in the event’s four-decade history.

Burning Man has hired a search firm to assist with finding a new CEO, with the organization anticipating that it will field candidates from the global Burning Man community and beyond. Anyone can put themselves or someone else forward for the role at executivesearch@burningman.org.

Goodell first went to Burning Man in 1995 — the sixth year the gathering happened in the Black Rock Desert after its early years on San Francisco’s Baker Beach. She helped found the Burning Man Project organization in 1997 and has been CEO since 2013. The organization now has 130 full-time employees.

Goodell shepherded Burning Man through an acutely challenging era. Burning Man co-founder Larry Harvey died in 2018, and the Black Rock City event was canceled in 2020 and 2021 due to the pandemic. This gathering subsequently experienced several difficult years due to weather, most notably a rain event in 2023, along with funding challenges that led the organization to launch a $20 million fundraising push in 2024.

Goodell says that as she exits the role, the next CEO “is inheriting a company and an organization that’s financially stable, that is behaving very responsibly about managing costs.” She notes that attendance at Black Rock City 2026 was roughly 64,000, a number the organization plans on capping going forward.

“If we look at what’s happening around the world and the culture, it doesn’t make sense to keep pretending that we could be at 78,000 [attendees] while gas prices are what they are, and Canadians don’t want to come to America,” says Goodell. “We need to be able to plan for expenses, size, volunteers, leadership, ice sales, etc. Why not just cap it at a place we found really amazing this year?”

Goodell also notes that the organization is “poised to start an endowment… Philanthropy is our cutting edge.”

Here, she talks about her decision to step down from the CEO role, and what comes next.

When I took over the role that my co-founders asked me to [take] in January of 2013, the whole point was that we were only going to run it as founder-led for as long as it took for the DNA of the organization to take on the instinct that the founders had themselves built through our collaboration and work together. It was always, always going to be that.

And why now — mostly because it’s time. It’s really easy for me to see that the work I’ve done is taking hold. I’ve been working on this [exit] since about 2022, 2021, right at the tail end of the pandemic when we had time on our hands, and I really looked at what we were doing inside the organization to actually prepare us for it… For me, it was more that during the pandemic we had the time to reflect and to create the organization in a way that makes it feel like we’re on the road to success. That the mission is actually not just Black Rock City. That people are getting it. And that’s what we’re seeing. As you wrote about in 2024, the regionals are now doing Burning Man culture more than what’s happening in Black Rock City — and that’s the win.

What is the next CEO inheriting? What’s the state of the org going into 2027?

The org, the event and the culture are all in great shape. The need to bring Burning Man culture out into the world is as strong as ever. The event did fabulously this year. We expect to have an event next year that’ll be about the same size. You’ll hear announcements about how we will [plan for] size and ticket sales in the near future. Nothing smaller, nothing bigger seems like the right size for where we’re at. And the organization structurally is in fabulous shape.

The last time we spoke was November of 2024, when the $20 million fundraiser was kicking off. Where are you financially, and what will the next person be inheriting in terms of finances?

The next person is inheriting a company and an organization that’s financially stable, that is behaving very responsibly about managing costs. It’s leaning into the opportunity that philanthropy provides us, so that we can make ticket prices available to a wide variety of people. They’ll be inheriting an organization that is leaning into the real mission, which is to bring Burning Man out into the world and managing the resources towards both the endeavors of Black Rock City and being out in the world.

2024 was hard — we did not hit our mark. [In] 2025 we hit our mark, we ended up with money on the books. This year we will hit our mark; it does not look like 2024… We are not in a state of crisis at all. We don’t leave in states of crisis.

The last years have been difficult, to say the least, with the pandemic forcing two years of cancellations, then a very hot 2022 and a major rain event in 2023. How do you reflect on those crises and your role in being there through them?

If you look at my tenure from 2013 and the first number of years, there’s really no crisis you can point to until Larry passes away. And then you end up with this series of challenges, one after another.

The whole point of Burning Man culture is to continue bringing in new people. Stepping aside for the next leader, the next generation. I’m talking the talk. We’ve brought the Rising Sparks up. [Editor’s note: Rising Sparks is a collective focused on bringing a new generation of artists, leaders and other Burners into Burning Man.] I’m the one that found the gal. I met this 22-year-old who comes and says, “I want to crack the code — I don’t understand why my generation isn’t here.” We did see an increase in young people this year.

When I spoke to a woman recently here in San Francisco, she said, “Yeah, we came this year, but we were 25 [people], and we’re normally 40,” and I said, “What are you doing to recruit the next generation?” Thunderdome didn’t come this year, and one of the things she said was, “We’re getting older and having babies and don’t feel like coming.”

So I don’t know why I should stay on any longer rather than find the opportunity to mentor. If we do this right, I’m a mentor. Just like we want when we turn over good leadership. There’s no other festival that proactively looks to its community to imagine how they want to step in. I’ve been watching for the right timing for me to step aside. It’s when I’m not tired, when I’m not done, when I’m ready to be a mentor and support the next generation.

What qualities does the ideal CEO candidate need?

When we started talking about this, people were like, “Oh my god, we’re never going to find anybody.” And I said, “I don’t necessarily think that’s true.” People keep over-indexing that I’m a founder, but it’s more about finding leadership to run the organization that’s dug into the culture. It’s someone who brings people together, can make clear decisions that advance the mission, and is enthusiastic about philanthropy. It could be someone who runs a nonprofit or a business — I do not think we need nonprofit experience specifically. I think you need deep cultural experience. And not necessarily all Burning Man. If you understand culture and you understand how to hold a space for culture, you’ll do well.

You’ve gotten so much criticism over the years, and the Burning Man documentary that was released in July included footage of Burners talking about wanting you to leave the CEO role. How tough does one need to be to be in this role?

The next person is definitely going to have to have thick skin. I’ve been here long enough to see waves of feedback, whether toward me, or other founders, or leadership in different departments. One has to be firm in understanding what you’re here for, what your values are, and be committed to the big picture — not take it personally. I really, really rarely take it personally. I know that I’ve done a good job.

And you’ve got to be authentic. Anybody who comes to this job with ego and thinks they’re going to do it to be famous is going to hurt themselves. But if you come to it to be of service for something that is truly transformative — the people in the organization, the volunteers, the temporary employees, all of the community — that is the kind of person who needs to look at this.

Who will have input in deciding on the next CEO?

The board has a search committee with five board members leading it. They’re working with a search firm. The plan is to have candidates vetted by board members first, and then staff, including myself, will all have input. But I’m not a decision maker. I trust the process. The board knows that if they put someone in place that the staff aren’t excited about, that’s going to be like organ rejection. You don’t want that.

What’s been the most difficult decision you’ve made during your tenure as CEO?

Canceling the event in 2021. That was the hardest decision because it took a long time to get there, and it had factors I couldn’t control. In 2021, we were all starting to get access to vaccines, and there was a lot of pressure to vaccinate participants and move forward. But when we had to make the decision — in March and April of 2021 — children still weren’t getting vaccinated, and our international participants couldn’t come.

And then there was a divide right down the community — employees, extended volunteer leadership, theme camp leadership. Some camps said, “No way, we won’t do it.” Other camps petitioned us and said, “We need this.” Board members were saying, “Are you kidding? We need the hope. We need the community. We need to build together.” So listening to both ends of that strong opinion, while knowing we’re not going to do an event without children or without international people — that was the hardest part. I was so anxious. Not because of making the decision, as much as the energy required to really listen to all sides.

I’m really, really proud of the honor of being part of this — the opportunity to meet Larry at a time when I could join the others and we could collaborate, when we were all in it together. I used to tell people: You get the short straw, you do it for a while, until you can hand it off to the next short straw. One of my best decisions was to quit my other job and do this work.

Is there anything else that you want to accomplish before you formally step down?

I feel very good about what I’ve accomplished. I don’t feel anything is left undone. I’m going to continue leading, inspiring the staff and listening to the community and building foundations for the future… to continue operating in a way that helps us all keep asking questions and listening to ideas from the community, particularly in some of the operations for the event. How do you run the gate better? How do you have less of a wait? Do you design the city differently? Do you change the date of the event? All of those are setting foundations of the future.

What do you want people to know going into this transition?

I think the most interesting thing is that this has never really been done before. I’m trying to set a good example — setting a template for how this can happen within this community and this organization. Why not create more space for people to step in and bring their ideas, their innovation? That’s the main goal. I’m not going anywhere. There’s no buyout. There’s no exit strategy in the traditional sense. This is the exit strategy.

Your email address will not be published. Required fields are marked *

Viral GTA 6 Competitor Gets Promising Anti-Cheat Update

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

Rob Run is a free-to-play crime sandbox where you participate in bank robberies, raids, shootouts, and risk losing everything in lobbies of up to 5,000 players. Create your own hideout, upgrade your base, and even join a clan to become the most powerful crime family ever seen. It has a familiar progression system among fans of survival games, thanks to the presence of server wipes, allowing you to write fresh stories every time.

Not only that, but Rob Run has voice chat, so all players can communicate out in the world, set up RP scenarios, and share hilarious encounters.

Grand Theft Auto 6 delivers an enormous open world, a gripping story, a vibrant Vice City and an astonishing range of activities.

If you’re not playing GTA 6 or you’re waiting for the new era of GTA Online to begin, Rob Run is a solid alternative. However, the biggest criminals right now are the cheaters who are ruining games for everybody else.

At the time of writing, Rob Run’s Steam reviews sit at “Mixed,” with the vast majority of negative reviews pointing out the prevalence of cheaters. In response, Kefir has announced upcoming measures in an attempt to nip the problem in the bud.

The team behind Rob Run is prioritizing strengthening its defenses against cheaters, so much so that an upcoming update that’s expected to deploy new in-game mechanics has been temporarily delayed. As well as this, discussions are being had about increased security measures with the Easy Anti-Cheat team, developing their own cheat-detection tools, and expanding the in-house anti-cheat team to deal with the onslaught of hackers.

According to Kefir, they have banned over 200,000 accounts so far, but “we know that in a free-to-play game, a standard ban isn't enough — cheaters can quickly create a new account and jump right back in. To combat this, we’re exploring hardware bans. Because separate servers with stricter access requirements can be launched much faster, we plan to roll those out first.”

The first planned solution is to make server access require either a minimum amount of jail time or a certain total of in-game purchases. Unlike the majority of other crime games, jail time is actually something you want to grow in Rob Run. The more wealth you have when the wipe sweeps in, the longer your prison sentence will be.

Players on these restricted servers will be matchmade separately from standard servers, including in Looty Bay. Depending on the minimum amount of jail time required, cheaters could be less incentivized by having to go through quite the grind before reaching regular lobbies.

The team behind Rob Run is prioritizing strengthening their defenses against cheaters, so much so that an upcoming update that’s expected to deploy new in-game mechanics has been temporarily delayed.

Of course, there’s a lot more work to be done in order to make Rob Run a zone free of cheaters. This isn’t Kefir’s first rodeo with hackers either, as their mobile game, Last Day on Earth: Survival, has also had its fair share of cheaters.

Hopefully, Kefir’s efforts pay off, but cheating remains one of the biggest problems in multiplayer gaming, especially in free-to-play titles. Counter-Strike 2 and Call of Duty: Warzone are just two such games that have been grappling with cheaters over the years. Cheat providers continue to evolve, despite developers rolling out new anti-cheat updates.

On November 17, Rob Run peaked at 549,661 concurrent players on Steam according to SteamDB. After such a strong start, it would be a real shame if the game is unable to reach its full potential due to cheaters holding it back. So far, the developers have kept communication open with players, which does bode well through these struggles.

GTA 6 leaker Arion Kurtaj was sentenced to seven years in prison on November 23, but walked free on licence after time already spent in custody.

Flipping a House Isn’t Paying Like It Used To. Is Renting the Better Bet?

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The typical home flip generated just $60,526 in gross profit in the second quarter of 2026, down from $71,000 a year earlier, according to new data—before accounting for renovation costs or other expenses.

It's a shrinking cushion for investors who have long relied on a simple formula to fuel their business: Buy low, apply elbow grase, and sell high. But with elevated borrowing costs and renovation expenses eating into margins, there's much less room than there used to be for anything to go wrong.

The report, from real estate data analytics company ATTOM, found that flipping returns have been declining for two years, with the typical gross return falling to 21.5%, from 27.6% a year earlier.

"Flippers are still making money in most markets, but the typical return continues to narrow," Rob Barber, CEO of ATTOM, said via press release. "The second-quarter numbers continue the general downward trend in profit margins and gross profits we have seen over the past two years."

So, why not skip the resale altogether and rent out the property instead?

After all, the promise of passive income—collecting rent every month while building equity—helped fuel a wave of small investors buying up homes earlier this decade when borrowing costs were low and rents were on a meteoric rise.

But the economics have changed for landlords, too.

Potential rental yields declined in more than half of U.S. counties with comparable data between 2025 and 2026, according to separate ATTOM research. That's to say nothing of the work involved in managing a rental property, which, as Realtor.com® previously reported, has proved to be anything but passive.

In a market defined by high carrying costs and shrinking returns, the better strategy may come down to which risks an investor can afford to weather—and for how long.

Despite shrinking profits, an index of fix-and-flip market conditions remained in expansion territory in the third quarter of 2026, according to a November survey of roughly 280 investors from John Burns Research and Consulting and Kiavi.

Some 66% of respondents reported selling most of their properties over the previous 12 months in line with their initial after-repair value (ARV) estimates—the amount a property is expected to fetch after renovations.

“Flipping can be profitable in a hot market with limited inventory, where homes sell quickly and buyers pay a premium for updated properties,” explains Realtor.com economist Jiayi Xu.

By purchasing below market value and making improvements that buyers look for, investors can boost the home's after-repair value and pocket the difference. In the right conditions, that means faster returns than holding the property long term.

And the slowdown may be creating opportunities to buy at a deeper discount.

Competition for fixer-uppers has eased in some markets, particularly Texas and Florida, where 44% and 37% of surveyed investors, respectively, reported less competition than usual, according to the November survey.

"Fewer bidders doesn't make every deal a good one," cautions Charles Goodwin, vice president of investor mortgage sales at Figure, Kiavi's parent company. “What it does is give well-capitalized investors with a defined exit strategy more room to be patient and negotiate. That's where experienced operators tend to separate themselves."

The trouble is, it's becoming harder to accurately predict what that exit will pay.

Four in 10 surveyed flippers reported closing sales at lower prices than a year earlier, according to the November John Burns–Kiavi survey. Meanwhile, 20% said most of their properties sold below their initial ARV estimates.

Among those who fell short of their targets, 84% said they had overestimated the eventual sale price, while 57% reported underestimating their costs.

Goodwin warns against basing resale projections on comparable sales from a stronger market.

"If a deal only works with last spring's comps, it probably doesn't work," he says.

And even when investors get their resale estimates right, renovations can consume a substantial share of the proceeds.

The average renovation cost was approximately $72,000 per flipped home in the third quarter of 2026, down from $80,000 a year earlier. Yet renovations still consumed a larger share of the average sale price, rising from 16% to 17%, according to the survey.

Financing adds another layer of risk, particularly when a project takes longer than expected.

The average effective interest rate reported by surveyed flippers who obtained new financing was 9.3% in the third quarter. At that rate, an investor carrying a hypothetical $320,000 interest-only loan would spend roughly $2,480 in interest for each additional month before selling.

Just one extra month would wipe out more than three times the $800 saved over a six-month hold by securing a loan at 9.3% rather than the previous year's 9.8% rate, according to the survey's calculations.

And even when a flip does succeed, Xu notes that taxes can reduce the final profit. Properties acquired primarily for resale as part of a flipping business generally generate ordinary income rather than qualifying for preferential long-term capital gains rates.

With strong headwinds to flipping a property, renting out a fixer-upper can offer a better risk exposure, especially for investors prepared to hold the property long term, according to Xu.

“If you prefer steady income, long-term wealth building, and lower risk, renting is generally the wiser choice,” she says.

Over time, owners may also benefit from property appreciation and tax deductions associated with operating a rental, making the rental path more attractive for those focused on building wealth.

And while asking rents were in decline for more than three years, the market is beginning to stabilize. The median asking rent rose 0.1% year over year to $1,692 in October 2026, its first annual increase since June 2023, according to the latest rental report from Realtor.com

Of course, renting out a fixer upper isn't entirely without risk.

Potential gross rental yields for three-bedroom properties declined in 54.8% of U.S. counties with comparable data between 2025 and 2026, according to ATTOM's annual single-family rental report.

"Even though rents and wages are rising in many markets, record-high home prices are compressing yields," says Barber.

And those projected yields don't account for the ongoing costs of owning and maintaining the property.

Katie Lyon, a rental property owner and host of the Landlord Diaries podcast, previously told Realtor.com that even profitable long-term rentals can leave landlords with little room for unexpected expenses.

"If the water heater goes out, you're back three years of cash flow," she says.

A simple benchmark can help would-be investors gauge whether a rental deal makes sense. The “1% Rule" suggests that monthly gross rent should equal roughly 1% of the purchase price.

When it comes to fixer-uppers, seasoned investors rely on a similar guiding principle. The "70% Rule," advises paying no more than 70% of a property's after-repair value, minus estimated renovation costs.

Ignoring this benchmark can quickly erode profits, especially in today's market where flip margins are already thin, warns Ian Hart, a seasoned housing investor and founder of Barndominium.org.

And the latest returns suggest that buying at the lowest possible price isn't necessarily the answer.

Homes purchased for $100,000 to $200,000 generated a typical gross flipping return of 28% in the second quarter of 2026, according to ATTOM. By contrast, properties acquired for $50,000 or less generated a typical gross loss of $15,000 before renovation expenses.

The November John Burns–Kiavi survey offers another measure of how cautious investors have become: About two-thirds of respondents said they wouldn't pay more than 70% of a property's estimated ARV, while the average maximum was just 67%.

Perhaps most importantly, though, buyers should look for exit flexibility. The best fixer-upper deals are those that work in more than one scenario—whether as a flip in a hot resale market or a rental in an area with strong tenant demand.

Allaire Conte is a senior advice writer covering real estate and personal finance trends. She previously served as deputy editor of home services at CNN Underscored Money and was a lead writer at Orchard, where she simplified complex real estate topics for everyday readers. She holds an MFA in Nonfiction Writing from Columbia University and a BFA in Writing, Literature, and Publishing from Emerson College. When she’s not writing about homeownership hurdles and housing market shifts, she’s biking around Brooklyn or baking cakes for her friends.

Khanom Tom (Coconut Balls)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The first time writer Ali Domrongchai went to Thailand with her grandmother, they had a mission: eat as many khanom (the Thai word for desserts and small snacks) as possible. They wandered from market to market, weaving through the stalls and sharing everything that caught their eye. Their hands-down favorite was the khanom tom, which her grandmother grew up buying from a street vendor in Bangkok as an after-school treat. Traditionally made with both fresh coconut and coconut milk, the bite-size sweets are chewy, fragrant, and irresistible.

Thankfully, khanom tom are easy to pull together at home and require no special equipment or technique. Glutinous rice flour gives the dough a pleasantly bouncy texture, and a palm sugar and coconut filling imparts a rich, caramel-like sweetness. After a quick boil, each ball is rolled in shredded fresh coconut for a soft, snowy finish.

Tinting the dough is optional but highly recommended. If you desire colorful khanom tom, divide the dough in step 1 and try one of these natural dyes:
