<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/hhs-medicaid-state-directed-payment-savings-primarily-stem-slowing-growth" hreflang="en">HHS: Medicaid state-directed payment savings 'primarily' stem from slowing growth</a>

📅 2026-11-23  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
HHS: Medicaid state-directed payment savings 'primarily' stem from slowing growth" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Rural hospitals claim a relatively small portion of the state-directed payments being squeezed in coming years by 2025’s H.R. 1, with much of the legislation’s projected spending reductions the result of holding funds for those hospitals from growing beyond fiscal year 2025 levels, Department of Health and Human Services policy heads wrote in a new report.

The analysis, released Monday, paints a softer picture of the rate limits Republicans imposed on a supplemental Medicaid payment stream in last year’s broad budget reconciliation law, also referred to as the One Big Beautiful Bill Act or the Working Families Tax Cut Legislation.

Hospitals and other healthcare industry groups, as well as advocates and Democrats, have said the law’s billions in projected savings—from state-directed payment changes as well as other policy areas like Medicaid work requirements—are likely to devastate providers already operating at thin or negative margins.

Specifically, the Office of the Assistant Secretary for Planning and Evaluation (ASPE)’s analysis showed that an estimated 7.8% of Medicaid state-directed payments reached rural hospitals in FY2025, or $11.5 billion out of $148.3 billion. That rate is slightly above rural hospitals’ 7% share of total Medicaid hospital spending for that same fiscal year, as estimated by the CMS Office of the Actuary.

Scaling from that baseline in line with H.R. 1’s multi-year payment rate phasedown, with some cumulative adjustments, showed that total nominal cumulative spending on rural state-directed payments would decline by 42.9%, or $81.6 billion, from 2026 through 2035 under the bill. This rate is again slightly higher than the CMS Office of the Actuary’s 40.8% estimate for all affected hospitals, which ASPE said is the result of the higher average dollar weighted payment rates rural hospitals currently receive, “which means that rural providers have more room to reduce rates to meet the new limits of 100% to 110% of Medicare.”

The ASPE report’s bottom line, however, is that $75.2 billion of the $81.6 billion reduction rural hospitals face, or 92.1%, reflects projected increases over the FY2025 baseline of $11.5 billion. The remainder, about 7.9% of the projected change, is a reduction below FY2025’s estimated state-directed payments to rural hospitals.

HHS, in a release highlighting the report’s findings, said that the language used in government reports describing the full $81.6 billion 10-year spending reduction as “savings” misses the government’s primary goal of capping future growth.

“Counting every dollar of projected savings as a cut below existing funding gives the public the wrong picture. Most of these savings are not reductions below what the program spent in 2025,” HHS Chief Economist and Chief Regulatory Officer Casey Mulligan, who was one of the report’s coauthors, said in the release. “They reflect future spending increases that will no longer occur. Our analysis makes that distinction clear.”

H.R. 1’s multi-year scale down in state-directed payment rates is set to begin in 2028, and are expected to slash some hard-hit states’ Medicaid spending by up to a quarter of their total annual Medicaid spending, per one analysis. Hospitals have broadly criticized the policy changes while spending much of this year adjusting their operations to contend with the future limits supplemental revenue, expectations of increased uncompensated care costs and year-to-year expense pressures like wage and supply inflation.

Additionally, CMS earlier this year proposed state directed payment caps substantially more extensive than what was outlined in H.R. 1 and used as the basis for Monday’s ASPE report. That proposal would extend rate limits beyond the bill’s four outlined service areas (inpatient hospital services, outpatient hospital services, nursing facility services and qualified practitioner services at an academic medical center) to “all [state-directed payments]s for all services in all states.” Hospital groups have flatly opposed the proposal, which more than triples the 10-year estimated savings.

Lawmakers last year, when seeking votes, blunted H.R. 1's funding reductions for rural hospitals by inserting a five-year, $50 billion rural health fund into the legislation. Those funds have come with usage restrictions for states and providers, with critics also noting transparency concerns and the fund's relatively small scale compared to the bill's total projected impact on hospitals.

→ Baca artikel asli

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/provider-groups-file-lawsuit-against-hhs-over-new-ryan-white-funding-rules" hreflang="en">Court strikes down anti-trans Ryan White funding rules 'based in animus, loathing and discrimination'</a>

📅 2026-11-23  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
Court strikes down anti-trans Ryan White funding rules 'based in animus, loathing and discrimination'" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Medical associations and providers notched a legal victory over the federal government in a court ruling vacating federal funding requirements for Ryan White clinics involving gender-affirming care.

Those restrictions would have required recipients of Ryan White HIV/AIDS Program funds to not acknowledge the identities of transgender patients or use funds for gender-affirming care.

It was challenged by the HIV Medicine Association of the Infectious Diseases Society of America (HIVMA), the American Academy of HIV Medicine and the International Association of Providers of AIDS Care in June (see that coverage below), who argued that the policy was contrary to Congress' intent when establishing the program under law in 1991, lacked sufficient explanation and discriminated against transgender people in violation of the First and Fifth Amendments.

The Nov. 19 decision from Judge William G. Young, of the U.S. District Court for the District of Massachusetts, prohibits the government from opposing its conditions in regard to all federal funding and permanently blocks any similar policies from being implemented by associational plaintifs' members, "which covers virtually every HIV care provider in the United States," said Lambda Legal, which acted as co-counsel for the plaintiffs.

"The ruling is a decisive and important victory for transgender people with HIV and protects the ability of physicians and other health care professionals to offer evidence-based care without political interference," Philip Bolduc, M.D., chair of HIVMA, said in a statement reacting to the decision.

Young's opinion and order opened with an acknowledgement that there are "sincere and deeply felt views" regarding transgender identity, and that the issue at question was of legal regulation rather than "the propriety of individual gender expression."

Still, the judge concluded his opinion by writing that the administration's conditions "are based in animus, loathing and discrimination. They do not stand for justice—in fact, they defy it. They are contrary to the laws of this nation and cannot continue to stand."

A group of medical associations and providers has filed a lawsuit challenging new federal funding requirements for Ryan White clinics involving gender-affirming care.

The new requirements, issued in a recent HRSA Notice of Funding Opportunity, require recipients of the Ryan White HIV/AIDS Program funds to not acknowledge the identities of transgender patients or use funds for gender-affirming care. The restrictions undermine the very intent of the program, the plaintiffs say, and violate anti-discrimination components of the Fifth Amendment.

“The Ryan White Program was created as a public health intervention to address the needs of low-income, vulnerable populations who otherwise would not have access to life-saving care,” Melanie Thompson, M.D., co-chair of a primary care guidance panel for the HIV Medicine Association, said in a press release. “It is crucial that Ryan White clinicians be allowed to continue to deliver the most scientifically appropriate treatments for all patients, including transgender individuals, without discrimination if we are to improve health outcomes for all and end the U.S. HIV epidemic."

The lawsuit alleges (PDF) that HHS and HRSA violated the Administrative Procedure Act by exceeding their statutory authority and imposing restrictions that conflict with the Ryan White statute. The plaintiffs ask the court to block enforcement of the funding restrictions.

The case was filed by Lambda Legal and HSF Kramer on behalf of the American Academy of HIV Medicine, the HIV Medicine Association and the International Association of Providers of AIDS Care, plus two providers.

A preliminary hearing will likely be scheduled in a few weeks. Lambda Legal is “cautiously optimistic” about the future of the case, José Abrigo, senior attorney and HIV project director at Lambda Legal, told reporters on a webinar on Thursday afternoon. He called the administration’s actions “unlawful,” and said gender-affirming care in this context includes all types of care, including primary care and mental healthcare.

Specifically, the HRSA Notice of Funding Opportunity reads: “Discretionary awards shall not be used to fund, promote, encourage, subsidize, or facilitate … denial by the recipient of the sex binary in humans, or the belief that sex is a chosen or mutable characteristic.”

Some providers are already preemptively complying with the restrictions, Abrigo said, particularly in Tennessee and Kentucky. It is unclear how many other providers will also stop gender-affirming care. In certain cases, it’s up to individual states or departments of health to enforce the restrictions. It’s possible that medical records could be audited and reviewed for indications of pronouns, per Thompson. “It does matter what someone’s gender and pronouns are listed as,” Thompson said on the webinar, explaining that medical records are shared between providers and help ensure affirming care.

If a clinic is found to be non-compliant, the repercussions are steep, Thompson added. “The penalty for being found out is severe, and puts an entire program’s funding in jeopardy,” she said on the webinar.

Forty percent of American Academy of HIV Medicine members rely on Ryan White funding, of which 40% voluntarily report providing gender-affirming medical care. For low-income trans individuals with HIV, there aren’t many alternative options, per Abrigo. They may seek care through private insurance or potentially through Medicaid, but typically the population relies on Ryan White clinics for services.

Gender-affirming care restrictions under the Trump administration have been commonplace. Over 40 hospitals have reportedly stopped some gender-affirming care services for minors in response to pressure. Last week, the Cleveland Clinic promised the Department of Justice (DOJ) to commit $2 million to detransitioning services and a “decades-long” agreement not to perform gender-affirming care on minors. Delivering most of these services is currently illegal for minors under Ohio state law. Last month, DOJ struck a similar deal with Texas Children’s.

For months, experts have been sounding the alarm about the U.S. HIV response slipping into dangerous instability. In April, the International Association of Providers of AIDS Care (IAPAC) released a data brief finding that 61% of providers have reported at least one HIV service disruption and 68% have seen operational changes such as staffing reductions, service cutbacks and program closures. This was attributed to a combination of policy decisions, funding cuts and social determinants like loss of insurance coverage.

“It’s a catastrophe in the making," José M. Zúñiga, AIPAC's president and CEO, told reporters on the webinar. "It was already happening. This adds fuel."

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Employer Provided IUL [Indexed Universal Life]

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

It is not all bad. Think of it as a fixed income investment. But be careful about the LIRP pitches. The problem with using an IUL for retirement is that the Cost of Insurance (COI) gets really expensive after age 60. However, you are not stuck. If you own the policy, you can do a 1035 exchange into an annuity. Make it a direct carrier-to-carrier transfer with no policy loan outstanding, so you avoid the boot rule (cash or loan relief in an exchange is taxable up to the amount of gain).

Once the money is in the annuity, withdrawals come out gain first. If you retire early, you can withdraw the gains and pay the taxes during your bridge years, then annuitize once you only have basis left. The basis comes back tax free, so this can be more tax efficient than pulling money out of the policy itself.

You might want to check out the IUL Experiment website for more details on how IULs work.

Statistics: Posted by Harmanic — Sun Nov 22, 2026 9:55 am

One Splurge Makes a Bathroom Look Expensive – Save on the Finishes Around It

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

In a bathroom remodel, put your splurge into one feature you’ll see or touch every day, like the vanity top or showerhead, and buy budget versions of the other finishes. Don’t skimp on what the walls hide, like the waterproofing behind the tile and the valve behind the shower handle, since fixing either later means opening a finished wall.

A bathroom looks expensive because of what the eye lands on first, like a stone vanity top, a rainfall shower, or one wall of standout tile. Premium material on every other surface raises the bill without changing that first impression.

Choose the splurge by how often you’ll use it. You touch the vanity top and stand under the showerhead every day.

Everywhere else, these budget choices hold up:

One swap can pay for the splurge. Say a couple wants a $1,200 quartz vanity top, and their quote prices 120 square feet of marble tile at $15 a square foot, or $1,800 before labor. Porcelain that looks like marble, at $2 a square foot, costs $240 for the same space, a $1,560 saving that pays for the top with $360 left over. Refinishing a tub that’s still in good shape and shopping salvage stores cut the bill further.

Don’t cut corners on three things. Tile and grout let water through, so the waterproofing membrane behind them keeps the wall dry, and a failed one means tearing out the new tile. Replacing the valve behind the shower handle later means opening the wall it’s in. And the EPA advises a working exhaust fan vented straight outside, not into the attic, to clear the moisture that lets mold grow.

Ask the contractor to name the membrane, valve, and fan by brand and model in the quote, so none gets downgraded to lower the total.

Sci-Fi Cult Classic From 41 Years Ago Launches Kickstarter Campaign For Long-Awaited Sequel

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ryan Northrup is a Senior Staff Writer on ScreenRant's Movie & TV News team, where he has authored nearly 5,000 articles, including features and reviews. A graduate of McMaster University, Ryan began his career in public relations before attending Vancouver Film School. There, he built lasting industry connections and optioned a feature film screenplay.

Repo Man 2: The Wages of Beer could be on the horizon.

Directed by Alex Cox, Repo Man was released in 1984 and stars Emilio Estevez as a young punk recruited by a car repo agency to track down a Chevrolet Malibu with something mysterious in its trunk. The film became a cult classic due to its wacky story and punk roots.

In a recent interview with The Guardian, Cox confirms that he is now crowdfunding Repo Man 2. The filmmaker teases that the sequel is set in the world of driverless cars, and it features a drastically different role for the titular repo man. Cox shares the news when discussing whether he still believes in the line from the original: "The more you drive, the less intelligent you are." Check out the filmmaker's response below:

That’s something my wife said when we were still students at UCLA and, at the time, I did believe it. Now I think that if you’re not relying on driver-assist technology, driving is an interesting occupation. It enables you to focus on something in the distance rather than staring at a screen in front of you. Right now I’m crowdfunding Repo Man 2, set in a world of driverless cars. Instead of sneaking down people’s drives, hotwiring cars and running away, the repo man just sits at a computer, takes control and drives the car to the sheriff’s substation or repo yard. It’s a different world, but the same kind of malevolence.

Cox then delves into crowdfunding as a means of getting movies made, explaining that major studios and streamers are primarily attracted to popular established properties. The filmmaker says that even the original Repo Man only got made because of personal connections and happenstance, making crowdfunding the best way to get a proper sequel off the ground:

For independent film-makers, yes, because the studios and the big streamers are really only interested in franchises and massive IPs. They want to spend a lot of money, but they don’t really care what the film is. Often they don’t read the script. In the case of Repo Man, it was obvious Universal hadn’t read the script. It got greenlit because of some people Nesmith met in a bar. The people who will back a Kickstarter actually want to see the film.

IndieWire reported in early 2024 that the sequel would be called Repo Man 2: The Wages of Beer. Set after the events of the original movie, the follow-up would feature Otto having aged only 90 minutes, with Kiowa Gordon taking over the role from Estevez.

This original report shared that Cox would be presenting the sequel to buyers at the European Film Market in the hopes of securing pre-sale funding. The filmmaker's latest comments to The Guardian about crowdfunding suggest that his efforts in 2024 may not have been entirely successful.

The Kickstarter for Repo Man 2: The Wages of Beer reveals that, as of writing, $98,125 has been raised out of a $500,000 goal. There are 374 backers for the project so far, with 43 days left in the campaign. As an "all or nothing" campaign, the project will only be funded if it actually reaches its $500,000 goal by January 4. The Kickstarter page reveals more plot information about the follow-up, including a synopses for the three main acts:

REPO MAN 2: THE WAGES OF BEER is the story of Otto, who has aged only 90 minutes since we last saw him, yet has also journeyed far and wide in space and time and seen things we wouldn’t believe.

Act One finds Otto on the job in the near future. No longer does he leave the Repo Office. His long nights are spent at a computer, selecting cars from the endlessly scrolling LA Repo Roll, and seizing control of their onboard computers. In this way, he is able to harass and extort the drivers while directing their vehicles to the police station, or the Repo Yard, which is now run by the Rodriguez Brothers.

In Act Two, fleeing a workplace disaster, Otto decamps to space – as a stowaway on five Billionaire Philanthropists’ mission to Mars. Otto witnesses the tragic accidental deaths of all but one of these beloved Billionaires, while simultaneously trying to repo their vessel and return it to Earth.

Act Three brings Otto back to his home planet, where things have changed a bit.

While it remains to be seen whether Otto will return in a Repo Man sequel, the original movie remains a cult favorite. The film features a cast that includes Harry Dean Stanton, Tracey Walter, Olivia Barash, and Sy Richardson. It was a hit with critics, sporting a 92% on Rotten Tomatoes, with the Popcornmeter not too far behind at 78%.

The film was made on an estimated budget of $1.5 million, meaning the sequel would be making do with quite a bit less if the Kickstarter is successful. Repo Man grossed about $2.3 million, the majority of which came from North America.

The movie landscape of today is vastly different than it was in 1984, and it's not yet clear how much of an appetite there is for a Repo Man 2. With about a month and a half left to go for the campaign on Kickstarter, it seemingly won't be long before the sequel's fate is decided.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Review: Secrets Playa Blanca Costa Mujeres (Hyatt Inclusive)

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Of note, I was in Cancun, Mexico for business meetings at another resort but wanted to evaluate both this experience for our readers as well as the overall position of Hyatt in its ever-growing Inclusives collection. My stay was almost exactly 24 hours and solo which is uncharacteristic at this destination resort. My experience will likely differ from other guests due to those factors.

Secrets Playa Blanca Costa Mujeres is an adults-only resort in the Hyatt Inclusive Collection, and I toured it on a site visit in November 2026. It sits on a stretch of white-sand beach in Costa Mujeres, 12 miles north of Cancun. Cancun International Airport is the arrival airport. I could not confirm a single official transfer time from the property page. Tour listings commonly put the drive in a 30 to 45 minute range.

Address: Carretera Punta Sam – Isla Blanca, Mz 2, Lt 10, Costa Mujeres, Zona Continental Isla Mujeres, Quintana Roo 77400, Mexico

Google Maps: Secrets Playa Blanca Costa Mujeres

Hyatt is the best in the business at high end resort arrivals. I had given my name at the security gate and by the time I reached the lobby, the door to the vehicle was opened for me, I was greeted by name and a “welcome home”, a constant refrain throughout my stay. It’s a small thing and maybe cliché but it was effective. I was given a Globalist upgrade even though rules at all-inclusives differ from the rest of the chain. As my upgrade was in a Preferred room, I was brought into a private room at the front of the lobby where check-in was done for me.

The lobby is an open-air room under an arched woven ceiling, with a direct view out to the ocean.

The resort is spread across 10 buildings with 505 keys. Of those, 221 are Preferred Club keys with an elevated experience. My building (and others like it) had just five rooms on each floor and just five floors which made it feel boutique rather than massive. Every oceanfront room is Preferred Club, and so are the first two buildings on either side.

The room views follow a simple rule by floor. Every first floor room is a swim-out, floors two through four are tropical view, and the fifth floor is ocean view or ocean front. That makes booking easy to reason through: if you want to step off your patio into the water, go low, and if you want the sea, go high.

The walkways give a sense of grandeur and space. All of the flora is local, and in a touch I loved, the service rooms are named after the plants.

There are four pools, and each one has a unique characteristic. The Jungle pool is drinks only and hidden in the middle of the resort by trees. The Social pool runs activities throughout the day at this adults-only resort for those who want to participate. There is also an infinity pool on the edge of the beach, and then the Preferred Club pool for those staying in that category. These are separate from the first floor swim-outs.

The spa (hydrotherapy is included for Preferred guests) featured an outdoor circuit that included both hot and cold plunge pools, amongst other therapeutic services.

The Preferred Club pool was the most frequented, though some images below look more vacant due to rain that rolled through during my opportunity to collect media on my short stay.

The gym is open to every guest from 6 AM to 8 PM, and it is outfitted with Technogym equipment.

The spa is open 8 AM to 8 PM with 17 treatment cabins and a beauty salon, and hydrotherapy is included. That last part is the headline. The hydrotherapy space is ridiculous, easily the standout of the entire property for me, and it is the kind of amenity that would make me pick this resort over a competitor down the beach. There are indoor and outdoor showers as well.

The men’s changing room featured private showers with an option for indoor or outdoor experiences.

The theater seats 264 people and hosts cabaret and Mayan shows. There are also pickleball courts, which I am starting to think should be required equipment at any resort that wants a younger crowd as well as padel for the European set (and growing American interest.)

Secrets is an adults-only brand, and every room here comes with what the company calls Unlimited-Luxury. For a standard guest that means unlimited dining and top-shelf drinks, Wi-Fi, daytime activities and nightly entertainment, with tips and taxes included. Add 24-hour room service, a mini-bar refreshed daily (you can ask for just about anything you want in there), and pool and beach wait service for all guests.

The Preferred Club is an upgrade, and at this resort it is a big one because it owns the oceanfront. Secrets lists personalized check-in and check-out, afternoon hot and cold hors d’oeuvres, desserts and premium liquors, access to the Preferred Club lounge and beach bar, an upgraded mini-bar, upgraded bath amenities, butler service, Bali beds at the pool and beach reserved for club guests, and exclusive dining at the Preferred Club beach restaurant. Preferred also gives club guests exclusive access to an infinity pool and puts an oversized soaking tub on the terrace of the upgraded suites (at least with the oceanfronts).

I book a lot of Hyatt’s all-inclusives through Hyatt Privé rates, and this resort is one of 388 in the collection. Privé is Hyatt’s invitation-only program for travel advisors, so you only get the benefits when an approved advisor makes the booking, and the stay still earns World of Hyatt points and elite nights.

The all-inclusive version of Privé looks a lot different from a Park Hyatt booking. As I wrote when Hyatt’s all-inclusive resorts joined Privé, there is no room upgrade and no breakfast benefit, since every meal is already included. At this property, Privé guests receive a $100 resort credit per stay, a welcome amenity and letter, hydrotherapy access, and early check-in and late check-out based on availability.

Elite status matters here more than at most all-inclusives. Under World of Hyatt’s terms, Globalists get the best room available at check-in, including rooms with club access, at the resort’s discretion. Explorists are eligible for upgrades too, but not to standard suites or club rooms. Travelers who have stayed at Secrets resorts as Globalists report being designated Preferred Club guests even when they did not land in a club building, which unlocks the lounge, pool and beach areas. This was my experience on all accounts at the resort. Late checkout is 2 PM for Explorists and 4 PM for Globalists, subject to availability at resorts. It was able to honor my request for a late check-out, I did not arrive early enough to request the early check-in. Hyatt waives resort fees on eligible paid rates (at least for now) and award nights for Globalists, and on award nights only for every other tier, and Globalists also get free parking on free nights though in 2028 Globalists will have to select this as a preference. One catch is that Suite Upgrade Awards cannot be used at Secrets resorts, so you cannot lock in a suite with one here.

Weddings are big business here. The courtyard outside the theater is the setting, with the sunset behind it, and the resort hosts lots of them. The resort disclosed its occupancy to me but it wasn’t clear if it was for public consumption. It was shockingly high especially for a Thursday in mid-November. Over the holidays or spring break all the hotels are full but this is a period where I would have expected a lull. Still, despite being extremely full, it never felt crowded.

For meetings and groups, the convention space measures 9,800 square feet and splits into five sections. The boardroom was occupied at the time so I couldn’t include images of that.

Restaurants can be booked in advance through the Hyatt Inclusive Collection app, which I wish more all-inclusives offered. Nobody wants to find out at 7 PM that the restaurant they wanted is full and through the app this can be done well in advance of a guest’s arrival. The dress code in the restaurants is resort casual, though the patio does not require slacks and closed toed shoes for men, shorts are acceptable outside.

The restaurants include: Portofino (Italian), Bordeaux (French), Himitsu (pan-Asian, with sushi and teppanyaki), Oceana (seafood in an open-air setting), Barefoot Grill (light lunch), Market Café (the international buffet, which I noted on site as the Marketplace), Seaside Grill (a poolside grill), and Coco Café. The bars on that list include Rendezvous in the lobby, Barracuda on the beach, a swim-up bar, a beach bar, and the Preferred Club beach bar and lounge bar. There’s also a Sports Bar and Lounge next to the theater.

Oceana is the seafood restaurant, set in an open-air dining room with woven pendant lamps, macrame hangings and a pebble mosaic floor. I chose this for my one dinner on property during my brief stay.

I started with the lamb croquettes, which the menu serves with yogurt and sweet chili sauce, cashews, watercress and a pickled shallot salad, and the Iberian salad with fresh mozzarella, prosciutto, rucula, toasted pistachios, balsamic and roasted peach. A crusty loaf came with the meal.

For a main I had the coconut shrimp, served with parsley orzo pasta, coconut sauce and parmesan crispy, and finished with the mango cheesecake, which the menu plates with vanilla cream and a mango compote with honey and basil.

Market Café, the international buffet I noted as the Marketplace, serves breakfast until 11:30 AM. [TO CONFIRM: lunch and dinner service at Market Café]

Seaside Grill hosts the Preferred Club à la carte breakfast. The official dining page says breakfast there is served for Preferred Club guests only. I tried a number of dishes for lunch to report to our fine audience.

Rendezvous sits at the heart of the property with 360-degree access, so you can walk up to it from any direction.

Preferred Club guests get their own bar at the beach, and the Preferred Club lounge also has an indoor lounge for when the sun gets to be too much.

My room was a Preferred Oceanfront King, a single open room with a king bed against a macrame headboard wall, rattan pendant lights, a ceiling fan, and a daybed sofa facing a marble bistro table. The balcony with jacuzzi looks straight over the beach. In the bathroom, a freestanding tub sits under a wood-slat ceiling beside a walk-in rain shower with a pebble floor and a bench. The double vanity has vessel sinks, round mirrors and a wave-textured wall, and the shower is stocked with Le Labo Bergamote 22 shampoo, conditioner and shower gel. Le Labo is a confirmed winner for me.

I also had the chance to tour a master suites, all of which are oceanfront, and they are also available as swim-outs.

Every first floor room is a swim-out, opening onto the pool.

Secrets Playa Blanca Costa Mujeres is an incredible resort that somehow felt small when you were in your building but big enough to have everything. For my stay, I used 45,000 points on a no-notice booking to offset a $1,300/night stay. I have seen rates from $560 for off-season which is without question worth it. I loved the property and I look forward to returning with more time and my wife, but the adults-only nature of the property will make it a harder stay for us to achieve.

What do you think? Have you stayed at Secrets Playa Blanca Costa Mujeres?

‘Hunger Games: Sunrise On The Reaping’ $120M U.S. Opening Is Second Best Start For A Prequel After ‘Rogue One’; Lionsgate Franchise Returns To Fortune – Sunday Update

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

SUNDAY AM: Refresh for more…Lionsgate and Suzanne Collins accomplished what no other Harry Potter movie has pulled off at the box office: Seeing a preview/spinoff opening to north of $100M at the domestic B.O., which is exactly what their Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping did with a North American start of $120M.

In addition, Sunrise on the Reaping ranks as the second best U.S./Canada opening for a prequel after 2016’s Rogue One: A Star Wars Story which debuted to $155M.

As rich as Harry Potter was in its sequels, Warners wasn’t able to get Fantastic Beasts to shine greater than a $74.4M opening, which is what the first prequel/spinoff title opened to in 2016. Hence, the Fantastic Beast movies became industry comps (along with 2019’s Hobbs & Shaw with a $60M start) in regards to the ceilings of what a spinoff/prequel franchise could open to at the box office.

The reason why we’re banging this drum is because when Lionsgate opened The Hunger Games: Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes to a ho-hum $44.6M three years ago, it felt like the popularity of the Collins franchise had hit the bottom of the well, destined to live in a middling range for the rest of its days. Nope.

Thumbs up to Collins for getting the franchise back on track in the backstory of Woody Harrelson character Haymitch Abernathy, making the sixth novel in the 14-year old series feel like the biggest Games yet, and to Lionsgate Motion Picture Chair Adam Fogelson in the continued belief that Hunger Games could see a renaissance among its fanship. It also helped that Collins’ latest novel Sunrise on the Reaping sold way more, and was received with more warmth than Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes which debuted during Covid.

How Lionsgate got Sunrise on the Reaping to shine brighter than the sun: That teaser, which launched a year ago, was the second most-watched in the studio’s history after Michael with 109M global views in its first 24-hours. If you want to bridge the gap in a legacy franchise between old fans and a new generation, you have to honor the old with the new. As such, to make fans go bananas, it was leaked back in December that OG Hunger Games stars Jennifer Lawrence and Josh Hutcherson were reprising their characters in the prequel — a scene that fans weren’t treated to in the book. Lionsgate leaned into them and Harrelson in the marketing with appearances on The Tonight Show and Watch What Happens Live.

When Lionsgate announced the 16 individual castings, those yielded 541 million views and 35.2 million engagements, an early driver and indicator of fans excitement.

Overall, the marketing campaign for Sunrise on the Reaping generated 1 billion owned social views and 74 billion views of fan-generated content across relevant Hunger Games TikTok hashtags and 1.5 million new followers across official social channels. The pic ranked No. 2 on TikTok’s Trending Chart behind Avengers: Doomsday. There was renewed audience enthusiasm among women 17-34 with Sunrise on the Reaping notching a 36% first choice over Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes.

1.) Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping (LG) 4,018 theaters, Fri $56M Sat $36.3M Sun $27.7M 3-day $120M/Wk 1

2.) I Play Rocky (AMZ) 2,477 (+2,448) theaters Fri $3.8M (+1960%) Sat $3.2M Sun $2.2M 3-day $9.3M/Total $10.4M/Wk 3

6.) You Can See Everything (A24) 1,252 (-893) theaters Fri $686,5K Sat $1M Sun $788K 3-day $2.52M (-53%), Total $93.4M/Wk 6

7.) Wild Horse Nine (Sea) 2,000 (-175) theaters, Fri $680K (-54%) Sat $1.1M Sun $720K 3-day $2.5M (-44%) /Total $17.96M/Wk 3Global for the Martin McDonagh directed and written pic stands at $39M, with $21M from 37 territories with UK in the lead with a $4.8M cume.

SATURDAY AM: Lionsgate has gotten The Hunger Games franchise back in the $100M+ opening range that this Suzanne Collins IP is known for on the big screen as the sixth film, The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, is now posting a $120M North American start after a $56M Friday, which includes previews. In addition, the CinemaScore jumped from a B+ on the last film, Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes, to an A- here.

PostTrak exits have increased to an 80% definite recommend, and 93% positive. The pic is playing evenly throughout the nation, however, strong in South, South Central, Midwest and Mountain. PLFs and Imax are repping 47% of the weekend (15% Imax, 32% PLFs). AMC Lincoln Square is the highest grossing venue for the Francis Lawrence directed title at $168K as of Friday.

Walk-up business for Sunrise on the Reaping was 36% yesterday in PostTrak exits (meaning those who purchased tickets same day). That’s similar walk-up business to Wicked two years (37%), and higher than last year’s Wicked: For Good (26%), but it’s lower than Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes (47%). Realize though that movie didn’t have a majority of fans buying tickets in advance like Sunrise on the Reaping (54% to 64%).

Women at 68% remain the big turnout for the latest Hunger Games movie which is no surprise. Forty-nine percent of the women who saw the movie came with one friend or more to watch the film (similar to Songbirds & Snakes 50% who came with one to five people), while 22% came with a date, spouse or partner (same as the 2023 film). The overall audience at 64% bought tickets because it’s for a franchise they love, while 33% said it was because of the cast.

Diversity demos on Sunrise on the Reaping are 49% Caucasian, 27% Latino and Hispanic, 9% Black and 9% Asian American, which is similar to the previous pic’s 50% Caucasian, 24% Latino and Hispanic and 10% apiece for Black and Asian American audiences. The percentage of 18-34 year olds showing up remained huge at 74% for Sunrise versus 76% for Songbirds & Snakes.

Sunrise on the Reaping currently is the fourth best opening in the franchise, but will likely be the third best, overtaking 2014’s Hunger Games: Mockingjay Part 1 ($55.1M Friday/previews and $121.8M 3-day) by tomorrow. The top two domestic starts in the Collins series are 2013’s Catching Fire ($70.9M Friday/previews, $158M 3-day) and the original 2012 Hunger Games ($67.2M Friday/previews and $152.5M opening). Catching Fire stills stands as Lionsgate’s top opening at the domestic B.O.

Amazon MGM Studio’s I Play Rocky is coming in ligher than expected with $9.3M despite scoring a rare A+ CinemaScore (which no Rocky movie in CinemaScore history has done even though this pic is about the making of the 1976 Sylvester Stallone movie). Note the movie is older skewing with 52% over 45, and that audience will take their time to come out, likely discovering the movie later this week. The exits here are powerful enough with an 87% definite recommend. 59% to 41% male-to-female with 50% men over 25, 37% women over 25 and 9% men under 25 and 5% women under 25. Very strong positive scores and definite recommends in every single demo. Caucasian turnout at 57%, Latino and Hispanic at 22%, Black at 8% (giving pic a 100%), Asian American at 7%, and Native American at 7% (also a 100% score).

No major PLFs on this pic. The Peter Farrelly directed movie is playing best in the West, East and South Central. To give you an idea of how older skewing it is, the Cinemark Palace 21 in Boca Raton, FL is the leading single-location gross at $11.2K yesterday.

The entire theatrical marketplace is around $172.1M, just -5% off from a year ago when Wicked: For Good opened to a franchise best of $147M. With no Moana, Zootopia, or Wicked in the marketplace, there was some concern that we would hit a dry spell at the Thanksgiving box office, which would be unfortunate given the great resurgence we’ve had at the domestic box office, now coursing to $9B by the end the holiday with an eye at $10B by the end of the year. Thanks to Lionsgate, Sunrise on the Reaping is going to get us through. With Disney’s Hexed and Universal’s Focker-in-Law expected to post around $45M a piece over the Wednesday-Sunday stretch, it wouldn’t be shocking if Hunger Games takes the lead with $50M+ over the 5-day box office as the franchise typically rallies over the Thanksgiving holiday.

Focus Features/Studio Canal’s highly buzzed Andrew Scott Best Actor movie Elsinore is seeing a solid opening theater average of $40K from 6 theaters in NYC and LA for a 3-day of $240K. Friday was $140K with $33K coming from AMC Lincoln Square and LA’s AMC Grove with $32K. Both had Q&As boosting ticket sales. RT critics are at 87% certified fresh.

Sumerian’s acquisition of the Sundance Grand Jury Prize and Audience winning drama Josephine from Beth de Araújo is looking at an estimated $35K Friday and 3-day of $138K at four locations in NY and LA. AMC Burbank is leading the way with $15K yesterday. The Channing Tatum and Gemma Chan starring pic is 97% certified fresh with RT critics. Opening per theater for the weekend is $34,5K.

Mubi has the Andrey Zvyagintsev directed crime thriller Minotaur which is France’s international film Oscar entry. The pic did $52K yesterday for what looks to be an estimated $123K opening at six sites in NY, LA, Toronto, San Francisco and Chicago. LA and NY are driving the ship with the latter’s Angelika Film Center minting $21K on Friday. The pic won Cannes’ Grand Prize of the Festival. Pic is 96% certified fresh with critics.

FRIDAY MIDDAY: Lionsgate’s The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping is heading for the fourth highest opening for the Suzanne Collins franchise as of now with $110M stateside at 4,108 theaters after a Friday that’s $51M which includes those $21M previews. If the movie isn’t front-loaded, it has a shot at possibly becoming the third highest North American opening for the franchise.

The industry overseas outlook is $97M, which if that holds means a $206.7M global opening. That latter number is not according to Lionsgate. A high $80M+ foreign start is within reach.

Top Hunger Games openings at the North American B.O. belong to 2013’s Catching Fire ($70.9M Friday/previews, $158M 3-day); the original 2012 Hunger Games ($67.2M Friday/previews and $152.5M opening), and 2014’s Hunger Games: Mockingjay Part 1 ($55.1M Friday/previews and $121.8M 3-day). Sunrise on the Reaping is the sixth movie in the Collins’ franchise, the fifth directed by Francis Lawrence. Altogether the previous five movies count $3.3 billion at the worldwide box office.

Again, the jaw drop here is how this franchise turned its fortunes upward after hitting an all-time low in its opening three years ago with Hunger Games: Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes ($44.6M). Comps to other sixthquels are hard, and the opening jump between Songbirds & Sunrise is +147% — which is unheard of. The surge in domestic openings between Christopher Nolan’s Batman Begins and Dark Knight was +225%.

Among all openings in November at the domestic B.O., Sunrise on the Reaping is currently 13th in a list of 16 movies that have bowed to over $100M for the month.

RelishMix beams that the great word-of-mouth that the pic has “book loyalists showing up in full force, insisting this adaptation finally respects the source material. There’s trust running through the comments, ‘Director Francis Lawrence’s track record and the franchise’s unbroken prequel streak as reasons to believe this one sticks the landing,’ and, ‘We’ll need therapy after this,’ read as proof of the story’s emotional weight. Comments marvel, ‘Dang from Voldemort, Moriarty, Hades, and Ramses to President Snow, Ralph Fiennes catching literary villain roles like Pokémon.’ Others draw sharper comparison, saying ‘this is like the and/or of the Hunger Games I feel it, I know it, I read the book and they are going to break your heart.'”

Overall social media reach is 652M across TikTok, YouTube, Instagram, X and Facebook which is not far from The Odyssey‘s pre-opening reach at 680M and +66% above where Hunger Games: Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes was in its social media universe, bowing shortly after the actors strike ended (393M). That 652M reach for Sunrise on the Reaping is 95% above other action-sci-fi-fantasy sub-genre features.

“Hunger Games cast activation index clocks 21 of 24 cast/creators activated with 40.4M total fans,” adds RelishMix in their weekly report.

“Notably, Jennifer Lawrence and her newly rebooted Instagram with 9.2M fans activated a video from Fallon shot in an elevator at 30 Rock with 25+M views and 40+M views on the Fallon IG. Activations also include McKenna Grace at 13M, Maya Hawke at 10.4M, Elle Fanning at 7.7M, Josh Hutcherson at 4.2M, Glenn Close at 1.3M, Joseph Zada at 442K and Whitney Peak at 492K. As well, some cast-off-the grid, include Jesse Plemons, Ralph Fiennes, Woody Harrelson and Kieran Culkin.”

Peter Farrelly’s I Play Rocky from Amazon MGM Studios is looking at $10M in second place after a $3.9M Friday in its third frame 2,477 wide break and a running cume of $11M by EOD Sunday. The exits are excellent, so let’s hope there’s long legs on this Sylvester Stallone biopic which is 89% with Rotten Tomatoes critics and 98% with moviegoers.

No. 3 goes to the second weekend of Paramount’s Johnny Depp Ebenezer at 3,545 theaters with $2.1M today, a 3-day of $8M, -58%, and ten-day total by Sunday of $32.8M.

Fourth is Warner Bros’ The Cat in the Hat at 3,262 theaters, a third Friday of $1.7M, 3-day of $7.3M, -35%, and running cume of $47.5M.

Fifth is the third weekend of Gkids/Toho’s Godzilla Minus Zero with $2.9M, -66%, after a third Friday of $700K at 3,000 theaters. Running total by Sunday is $54.7M.

FRIDAY AM AFTER THURSDAY EXCLUSIVE: Lionsgate’s Francis Lawrence directed The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping came in with $21M in overall previews since Wednesday. As we told you, that’s the second best for the Suzanne Collins franchise behind Catching Fire‘s $25.2M in 2013 and the second best for 2026 behind Spider-Man: Brand New Day‘s $72M.

Note that all the movies that have posted $20M+ in previews went on to cross $100M+ in their opening weekends. This is a big deal for Lionsgate following its success with Michael, which went on to make $1 billion at the global box office, one of five movies to clear that threshold this year.

RELATED: The 65 Movies That Have Made More Than $1 Billion At The Global Box Office

Out of the gate Thursday night, Screen Engine and Rentrak’s PostTrak shows 79% “definite recommend” and 5 stars with a 92% positive for general audiences. Women showed up at 67%, with 38% females under 25 and 29% over 25. Biggest definite recommends are from women under 25 (89%), the 13-17 set (92%) and 18-24 demo (82%). The 18-34 showed up at a massive 77%. Close to 70% of Thursday night’s audience bought their tickets within the past week or more than a week ago.

Excitement among fans was apparent a year ago this week, when Lionsgate dropped the first trailer for Sunrise on the Reaping: It went on to clock 109M views in its first 24 hours, becoming the second-most viewed trailer for Lionsgate after Michael‘s 116M global view traffic.

RELATED: ‘The Hunger Games: Sunrise On The Reaping’ Review: Is Time Finally Running Out For The Dystopic Game Show Franchise?

There’s great audience buzz for Amazon MGM Studios/FilmNation’s I Play Rocky with 5 stars, 97% positive and a great 86% definite recommend from general audiences last night.

EXCLUSIVE: No Wicked, no Moana, no problem, because Lionsgate is saving the Thanksgiving week box office with The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, which is posting $21.25M in previews, the second-best ever for the franchise behind 2013’s Hunger Games: Catching Fire, which lit $25.2M in its previews. Sunrise‘s money is currently the second-best previews YTD after Sony’s Spider-Man: Brand New Day ($72M, $360M) and ahead of Universal’s The Odyssey ($17.6M previews, $123.5M opening).

RELATED: All The Best Picture Oscar Winners – Photo Gallery

Other previews that Sunrise on the Reaping is beating include Universal’s Wicked from two years ($19.2M; $112.5M opening); for the longest time we heard that the latest feature take of a Suzanne Collins novel was outstripping the presales of that female-skewing musical starring Ariana Grande and Cynthia Erivo. It’s also ahead of Lionsgate’s Michael ($12.6M previews, $97.2M opening), The Devil Wears Prada 2 ($10M, $76.7M opening) and light years ahead of the previous Hunger Games: The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes ($5.75M previews, $44.6M opening), which had its campaign impacted by the 2023 actors strike, not to mention the Collins novel about the origins of Coriolanus Snow wasn’t a fan fave. Sunrise on the Reaping was beloved by fans in its early story about the Woody Harrelson mentor character, Haymitch Abernathy.

When it comes to the all-time preview record for a Lionsgate title, that belongs to The Twilight Saga: Breaking Dawn – Part 2, which made $30.4M from midnight shows leading to a $141M opening back in mid-November 2012.

RELATED: All The Best Actress Oscar Winners – Photo Gallery

Now, Sunrise on the Reaping‘s advance money was minted with $5M via early access PLF showtimes on Wednesday, with another $16.25M collected tonight per sources. Catching Fire‘s back in the day began warming up with 10 p.m. Thursday night shows. Note that Wicked‘s previews were composed of Amazon promotional screenings, Wednesday night PLF shows and an $8M Thursday.

With a great preview start, it’s fair to say that a $100M opening is well in store for the net-$150M priced Sunrise on the Reaping, especially with a 95% audience score on Rotten Tomatoes, which is the best ever in the franchise ahead of Catching Fire (89%), Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes (89%), Hunger Games (81%), Mockingjay Part 2 (71%), and Mockingjay Part 1 (66%).

Overseas, industry projects have risen from a $70M forecast to $86M opening.

RELATED: All The Best Actor Oscar Winners – Photo Gallery

Critics aren’t hot on the fifth Hunger Games movie from director Francis Lawrence (the original 2012 movie was helmed by Gary Ross) with an RT score of 68% fresh. However, even if Katniss was shooting Cupid’s arrows at critics, they’d still play hard to get, for the best reviews in the series were a a while ago with Catching Fire (90% certified fresh) and Hunger Games (85% certified fresh). Ever since the Mockingjays (both at 70%), critics’ love for Hunger Games has waned with Songbirds and Snakes being the worst reviewed in franchise at 64%.

The Hunger Games franchise through its five previous movies has minted $3.3 billion at the global box office.

Inside a Secluded Cabin Hidden Right Beneath Big Sur’s Iconic Landmark

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A secluded retreat tucked beneath one of California’s most photographed landmarks in Big Sur has come on the market for $3.65 million.

The home at 39320 Coast Road sits in Bixby Canyon and is one of only seven residences behind a private gate.

“Millions recognize Bixby Bridge, yet very few will ever experience the landscape beneath it,” says listing agent Michael Hornig of the Strock Team at eXp Realty.

The canyon also comes with a literary history: In 1960, Jack Kerouac retreated to a cabin here owned by Lawrence Ferlinghetti, the poet and co-founder of City Lights Bookstore. His time in the canyon formed the basis for his 1962 novel, “Big Sur.” While this is not the original Kerouac cabin, it shares the same setting.

According to Hornig, the canyon has also been home to the film director Sam Peckinpah and the Disney illustrator Ron Dias.

“There’s an extraordinary feeling about this canyon,” Hornig says. “You’re surrounded by a landscape that inspired some of America’s most influential writers, and remarkably, much of that atmosphere of solitude and wilderness remains intact today.”

Built in 1984, the single-level home has three bedrooms, two full bathrooms, and 1,489 square feet of living space. Vaulted, open-beam ceilings, hardwood floors, and three wood-burning stoves characterize the interiors, as do large windows that frame the surrounding redwoods. A wraparound porch overlooks the forest.

“Luxury doesn’t always have to be expressed through scale or elaborate architecture,” Hornig says. “Here, the luxury is the relationship between the home and its surroundings.”

The property covers two parcels: a 0.79-acre residential lot and an adjacent 0.33-acre creekside lot. It is move-in ready, with a private well, septic system, and electricity. There is also a fenced backyard, storage shed, pasture space and parking for 10 vehicles, including EV charging.

Owners share access to a privately held canyon enclave of more than 140 acres. There, a creekside trail winds through redwoods and ferns to a secluded beach beneath the bridge.

“You can walk from your front door through a redwood forest, follow the creek toward the Pacific, and emerge beneath one of the most recognizable bridges in the world,” Hornig says.

Next to the property, Brazil Ranch adds about 1,200 acres of U.S. Forest Service land for hiking, hunting, and recreation. The historic Old Coast Road offers miles of biking, horseback riding, and backcountry exploration. Highway 1 is about five minutes away. Carmel, Monterey, and Big Sur’s restaurants and state parks are about 20 minutes away.

“To me, the greatest luxury here is the privilege of belonging to a landscape that most people will only ever admire from a distance,” Hornig says. "It's this contrast between one of California’s most recognizable landmarks and the hidden residential community beneath it."

Israel's Eisenkot condemns 'Jewish terrorism' in West Bank

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Israeli opposition leader Gadi Eisenkot has condemned a recent series of arson attacks in the occupied West Bank as "Jewish terrorism." Israeli authorities have opened an investigation into the incidents.

Israeli authorities were on Sunday investigating arson attacks and vandalism against Palestinian properties and vehicles in the occupied West Bank which Israeli opposition leader Gadi Eisenkot condemned as "Jewish terrorism."

Images posted on social media showed vehicles in flames and slogans written on walls reading: "In blood and fire Judea has fallen, in blood and fire Judea will rise again," using a biblical name for the West Bank.

"Settlers attacked the northern area on the outskirts of the village at around two in the morning," Jihad al-Qaq, a local activist, told the AFP news agency.

"They set fire to around eight vehicles and wrote racist slogans on the walls of houses."

Israeli soldiers and police were deployed to the towns of Deir as-Sudan, Deir Abu Mashaal, Kafr Malik and Al-Tuwani following the reports, with the Israel Defense Forces (IDF) saying it "strongly condemns" the acts.

The suspected arson attacks were condemned by Israeli opposition leader Gadi Eisenkot, who is engaged in post-election negotiations to form a government to replace that of Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu.

"The night of arson … is Jewish terrorism in every sense of the word, and it must be called what it is," he said.

"Sabotage by extremist groups setting fire to vehicles and homes undermines regional stability, endangers lives, and severely damages the national security and foreign relations of the State of Israel," he said. Eisenkot used the biblical terms, Judea and Samaria, to refer to the West Bank territories in question.

More than 500,000 Israelis live in West Bank settlements, which are illegal under international law, alongside around 3 million Palestinians.

The West Bank has been occupied by Israel since 1967 but attacks against Palestinians have surged in the wake of Hamas' October 7, 2023 attack on Israel. Since then, more than 1,100 Palestinians in the West Bank have been killed by Israeli soldiers or settlers, according to data from the Palestinian Health Ministry.

Local resident Ahmad Ghneimat told the Associated Press that violence had increased after several Israeli settler outposts sprang up in the area. He said settlers have stolen sheep and violently harassed olive harvesters.

"We woke up at 2:30 at night to the sound of explosions [and] seven cars on fire," said Muntasir al-Malik, another resident. "We came out of the houses and panicked."

Eisenkot called for "severe sanctions" to be imposed on the individuals responsible.

Eisenkot's labeling of the violent settlers as "terrorists" follows the use of the same term by the United States' ambassador to Israel, Mike Huckabee, in September. US officials usually reserve the term for Palestinian militants.

Inside the $7 Million Historic Georgia Estate Straight Out of ‘Practical Magic’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Tucked away along Pine Log Creek in Fairmount, GA, sits a nearly 71-acre historic estate that feels less like a residential listing and more like a Hollywood set.

Known as Yarborough Mill, the $7 million property features a restored late-1800s Queen Anne Victorian—a 4,500-square-foot former grist mill and plenty of period charm.

"It looks like something that belongs in 'Practical Magic'," says listing agent Logan Fitts with Mountain Sotheby's International Realty. “It is definitely the most historic property I've had the chance to work with, and with that it is just so unique.”

The property has several structures, but the highlights are the former grist mill and a late 1800s Queen Anne Victorian.

“Everything's original, but it's all been restored because it was not in perfect condition when [the sellers] bought it. It's a complete restore project and everything is true to period,” Fitts explains.

“The exterior of the main house, the colors she chose, the roof tiles, the architecture, and the landscaping. Everything there was intentional.”

The Victorian home has some Georgian features and has five bedrooms and three full bathrooms.

“It looks like it could be in The Gilded Age or something like that. There are a total of eight fireplaces in the house. There's parlors, there's dens and small living rooms, and little nooks and crannies throughout the house,” he says, adding the kitchen is updated appliance-wise, but still has character.

“[There are] stained glass windows throughout, and pressed tin ceilings throughout. It’s insanely intricate.”

But it’s another room that caught Fitts’ eye.

“They have an old solarium on the lower level that they turned into the coolest bathroom that I've ever seen,” he says.

“You have to be in there to really see it. If you’re entertaining in the house, anyone what visits or goes to that bathroom is just totally wowed. It just it feels like you're in a solarium, but it is a bathroom.”

This home has been the seller’s primary residence, with a three-bedroom and three-bathroom cottage for guests and others, especially during events. The seller has operated a small wedding business on the property.

“She's done it herself. It has been more of a passion for her. It's a wedding venue for small groups and the guest cottage is used as a bridal suite.”

The groom’s suite and game area are above the barn, and the 4,500-square foot former grist mill is the event space.

Fitts says most of the wedding ceremonies happen along the waterfront along Pine Log Creek, which creates a stunning backdrop.

“You pull up to a gate and [after] the gate opens, you go down this long winding driveway with rows of trees and the river that winds through the entire property to your right,” he explains. “Then you turn in up the bluff a little bit, and then it kind of opens up to what you would see in the olden days, like its own little village town.”

An old general store and smokehouse are also on the property. Fitts says the sellers saw a for sale listing in the newspaper at least 15 years ago, drove up to see it, fell in love with it, and then restored it.

“I was impressed with the story behind it [and] the amount of love that went into owning the property,” he says, adding there are two kinds of buyers who would be perfect for this estate.

“It could be a visionary entrepreneur that really wants to lean into making this a hospitality-based venue, spa, bed and breakfast, a true sanctuary. There's potential for growth,” Fitts explains, adding the buyer could build other structures throughout the orchard and make the main home a gathering space.

“It could be somebody's true entrepreneurial dream or it could just be someone that truly loves historical properties that wants to preserve what is there, love it with their family or for themselves, and just cherish it for what it is.”

Premium Upgrade? United Airlines Is Replacing Its Remaining Boeing 767s With 787 Dreamliners

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

On September 8, 1982, United Airlines brought the Boeing 767 into commercial service, revolutionizing its long-haul network with a twin-engine aircraft that could safely operate transatlantic routes dominated by quadjets. The airline still uses 53 of these legacy twinjets to anchor its transatlantic network from Newark Liberty International Airport (EWR) and Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) more than 40 years later. The current fleet consists of 16 stretched 767-400ER aircraft with an average age of 23 years and 37 older 767-300ER aircraft with an average age of 29.

United has historically relied on the 767 to serve European cities 'across the pond.' Now, United intends to retire all of these aircraft by 2030. To attract surging transatlantic travel demand without flooding routes with low-margin coach tickets, United is deploying 222-seat Elevated Boeing 787-9s featuring 99 premium seats, including 64 enclosed Polaris suites and 35 Premium Plus recliners, alongside stretched 787-10s for slot-constrained hubs like London Heathrow Airport (LHR).

The post-pandemic rise in demand for year-round leisure and business travel to Europe reshaped United's business model around a 'premiumized' cabin. The Dreamliner's wider composite fuselage accommodates spacious suites in the front of the cabin with a lower 6,000-foot (1,829-meter) cabin altitude and higher air humidity, which softens jet lag regardless of seat number. Combined with 25% better fuel efficiency over the 767, these upgrades let United expand premium capacity and passenger comfort on top European routes and lower operating costs.

Moving from the 1980s-era 767 to the 787 Dreamliner improves passenger appeal but also route flexibility. While the 767's flight range is limited to roughly 5,980 nautical miles (11,075 km), the next-gen 787-9 flies up to 7,565 nautical miles (14,010 km). Its faster Mach 0.85 cruise speed also shaves up to 50 minutes off eastbound Atlantic crossings, helping United meet tight European airport arrival slots while easily rotating aircraft onto Pacific routes on the West Coast for seasonal destinations.

In 2022, following a landmark order for 100 787s and an option to buy 100 more, United CEO Scott Kirby gave these remarks: "United emerged from the pandemic as the world's leading global airline and the flag carrier of the US. This order further solidifies our lead and creates new opportunities ... to connect more people to more places around the globe and deliver the best experience in the sky."

Stan Deal, president and CEO of Boeing Commercial Airplanes, also offered this comment: "With this investment in its future fleet, the 737. The Boeing team is honored by United's trust in our family of airplanes to connect people and transport cargo around the world for decades to come."

Evolving from aluminum to composite jets not only makes the passenger experience better but also opens up more freight revenue for UA. The 767-300ER maxes out at 412,000 pounds (186,880 kg). In comparison, United can transport up to 52,400 pounds (23,768 kg) of high-margin commercial freight in the belly on each transatlantic flight, with the 787-9's increased maximum takeoff weight of 560,000 pounds (254,011 kg). That's before all the other improvements the Dreamliner delivers.

Passengers traveling across the Atlantic will see immediate changes on the flightline and inside the cabin when the outdated 767s are retired in favor of 787 Dreamliners. The wider composite cabin significantly improves personal living space across all seating classes as compared to the tiny 1980s-era 767 configuration. While older 767s had open, doorless business seats and tight overhead bins, the forthcoming Elevated 787-9s feature enclosed Polaris suites with sliding privacy doors, wireless charging, and larger 4K TVs.

Economy class passengers also benefit from wider aisles, sculpted sidewalls, dynamic LED mood lighting to facilitate time-zone transitions, and oversized overhead bins that can carry each passenger's roll-aboard bags. Additionally, the 787's single-piece carbon-fiber body can withstand substantially higher internal pressure without experiencing metal fatigue, enabling the cabin to maintain a comfortable 6,000 feet (1,829 meters) of pressure with 16% air humidity.

Meanwhile, the 767 must maintain cabin pressure at the old standard of 8,000 feet (2,438 meters) in simulated elevation. It must also maintain air humidity below 10% to prevent metal joints from rusting, since a conventional riveted aluminum fuselage bends and stresses under high air pressure. After seven hours in the air overnight, passengers benefit from the increased blood oxygen level that the 787 delivers.

That means fewer headaches and substantially reduced jet lag thanks to the lower cabin altitude and additional moisture. Dreamliner windows are also 65% larger than those on the 767 and use electronic dimming controls rather than plastic pull blinds to manage sunlight. The Dreamliner cabin is also significantly quieter during takeoff and cruise thanks to sophisticated acoustic insulation surrounding the engine nacelles and the lack of noisy high-pressure air lines for sub-systems because of its all-electric architecture.

United Airlines has overtaken ANA as the world's largest Boeing 787 operator, with further Dreamliner expansion planned.

Despite UA's rapid trajectory of 'premiumization,' Boeing must resolve significant assembly delays at its North Charleston facility in order to support the airline's goals. In order to replace its 53 outdated 767s, United Airlines updated its backlog to a firm order for 150 Boeing 787 Dreamliners, with options for a further 50. However, production was delayed considerably by Federal Aviation Administration safety inspections following quality problems with composite materials. Due to these delays, United was obliged to reduce the number of Boeing aircraft it planned to deliver in the near future from 165 to just 63, delaying the majority of new widebody deliveries until after 2026.

In 2023, Kirby gave this update on the UA fleet: "Our planning and focus on the long term have helped us surge past other airlines that stood still. I'm convinced our strategy is the right one as we continue to add new, larger aircraft to take full advantage of our growing flying opportunities both internationally and domestically."

Meanwhile, Deal spoke for Boeing and added these remarks: "United's unprecedented commitment to the 787 Dreamliner family is a testament to the market-leading operating economics and reliability of Boeing's widebody jets. With the 787, United will enjoy all the synergies of operating one of the largest, most efficient fleets in the skies."

United will still be forced to spend millions of dollars maintaining the safe operation of 30-year-old aluminum aircraft while waiting years for delayed Dreamliners. High-time 767-300ER and 767-400ER aircraft are subjected to extensive, costly structural inspections by mechanics rather than being retired to desert storage. In order to make up for the timetable deficit, older aircraft, such as tail N641UA, which has nearly 100,000 flight hours and 20,000 landings, require expensive overhauls on their older General Electric engines in addition to thorough wing and tail scanning inspections.

After taking a $200 million financial hit from factory delays and plane groundings, United CEO Scott Kirby put together a backup plan. Along with leasing 35 Airbus A321neo jets to cover short-term domestic flights, Kirby kept United's delayed order for 45 Airbus A350-900 widebodies alive. Holding onto those European factory slots gives United a real alternative if Boeing's South Carolina assembly delays continue.

Get ready for Simple Flying's latest feature!

Moving from the Boeing 767-300ER to the 787-9 represents a major leap forward in jet engine design, which enables greater range and payload as well as better fuel burn rates. United's older 767s run on Pratt & Whitney PW4060 or General Electric CF6-80C2B6F engines built with 94-inch (239 cm) titanium fans and a 4.8:1 bypass ratio, producing around 60,200 pounds of thrust (268 kN). In contrast, the 787-9 uses General Electric GEnx-1B engines rated up to 75,000 pounds of thrust (334 kN).

The GEnx features 111-inch (282 cm) carbon-fiber fan blades, a lightweight composite fan case that cuts over 400 pounds (181 kg) per engine, and an 8.8:1 bypass ratio that trims fuel burn by over 20% while cutting nitrogen oxide emissions by 55%. Additionally, the two generations also differ in how each aircraft powers its internal systems.

In order to run the cabin air conditioning, start the engines, and heat the wings against ice, the 767 uses a conventional bleed-air configuration that draws hot compressed air above 392°F (200°C) straight from the engine cores, depriving the turbines of useful power. In order to improve engine efficiency and reduce cruise fuel consumption by about 4%, Boeing replaced the titanium air ducts on the 787 with an all-electric system powered by four engine-mounted generators that produce 1.4 megawatts of electricity.

Since the 767's body is made of conventional aluminum sheets welded over metal ribs, it is susceptible to rust and metal fatigue during thousands of pressurization cycles. In contrast, the 787 uses single-piece body sections and long, thin wings that flex upward by roughly 10 feet (3 meters) at the tips during flight to smooth out turbulence and reduce air drag at Mach 0.85. Strong carbon-fiber composites constitute half of the 787's structural weight.

If Your Social Security Benefit Is Above This Average, You're Ahead of Most Retirees

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Beating the national Social Security average is one thing, but whether that check actually covers retirement is a far tougher test.

By Kailey Hagen, CFP® – Nov 22, 2026 at 10:00AM EST

Where does your monthly benefit check sit compared to the rest of the country? As of October 2026, according to the Social Security Administration (SSA), the average monthly benefit for retired workers is $2,092.17. That amounts to about $25,106.04 per year. This single figure covers everyone from those who just claimed at 62 to retirees well into their 90s.

Averages depend heavily on who you are and when you claimed. For instance, the average for people aged 62 is $1,424.40 per month, while those aged 70 receive an average of $2,274.68. Gender also creates a significant gap in the final numbers.

Averages also vary between men and women across the entire retired-worker population. As of December 2025, according to the SSA, these were the monthly averages for men and women across all age groups. They reflect the combined impact of different career earnings and claiming choices.

Landing above the $2,092.17 benchmark isn't a matter of luck. The SSA calculates your benefit based on an average of your monthly wages across your 35 highest-earning years. It adjusts earlier wages for inflation and runs them through the primary insurance amount (PIA) formula. Higher lifetime earnings generally result in a larger monthly check.

Claiming age is the other primary lever for beating the average. Full retirement age (FRA) ranges from 66 to 67, depending on your birth year, and it's 67 for everyone born in 1960 or later. Claiming at age 62 can reduce your check by up to 30%, while waiting until 70 earns delayed retirement credits worth 8% per year.

While beating the average is helpful, it's rarely enough to cover a full retirement. Social Security is designed to replace only about 40% of your pre-retirement income. However, the average senior household headed by someone 65 or older spends $61,432 per year, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS). That's much higher than the $25,106.04 annual average benefit.

A 3.6% cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) will take effect in 2027 to help checks keep pace with inflation. That adjustment adds about $75 per month to the $2,092.17 average. Even with these raises, most retirees rely on savings or other income sources to bridge the gap between their benefits and their total living costs. Beating the Social Security average is not the goal; covering your actual retirement expenses is.

If you want to increase your odds of claiming an above-average benefit, there are three things you must do. First, work at least 35 years before you apply for Social Security. This will ensure you have no zero-income years included in your benefit calculation, which would weigh down your checks.

Second, do what you can to increase your income today. The larger your average income throughout your career, the larger your future Social Security checks will be.

Third, choose your claiming age carefully. Claiming Social Security early could make sense if you have a short life expectancy or don't have much in personal savings. Otherwise, you may get a larger lifetime benefit by delaying Social Security until your FRA or possibly until you qualify for your largest checks at age 70.

For more on how Social Security affects your retirement, see this guide.

Buy Electronics & Toys on Black Friday – Wait Until January for Treadmills & Sheets

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Buy TVs, laptops, tablets, small kitchen appliances, smart-home gear, and toys over Black Friday and Cyber Monday, when their deepest discounts land. Wait for January’s sales on treadmills and bedding, and check the price history of anything else on your list before you pay.

A Black Friday discount is worth taking only on something that won’t sell for less later in the year.

Price data from Consumer Reports (CR) shows that 46 kinds of products it tests reach their lowest prices in November, among them TVs, laptops, tablets, air fryers, coffee makers, blenders, smart speakers, thermostats, and security cameras. CR expects Black Friday and Cyber Monday to bring the best deals of the year.

Adobe, which analyzes online sales at U.S. retailers, expects this holiday season’s best deals on TVs, toys, and appliances to land on Black Friday, and the deepest discounts on computers on Cyber Monday. It forecasts discounts of up to 30% off list price on electronics and 29% on toys. Sporting goods should see their best deals a day earlier, on Thanksgiving.

Fitness equipment and bedding are January buys instead. January’s sales are sparser overall, CR notes, but they tend to bring good prices on treadmills and other fitness equipment. Many stores also run white sales then, bedding promotions with big markdowns on sheets and pillows. So a laptop on your holiday list is worth buying on Cyber Monday, while a treadmill on the same list can wait for January.

For anything else on your list, such as a sofa, look up its price history, which charts what the item has cost over recent months. Buy if the price sits at or near its recent low, and otherwise wait for the item’s own yearly sale season.

Before Thanksgiving, mark each item on your list as a Black Friday buy or a January buy, and set a price alert on the rest.
